Hook gaps > 0.5 ms total **40.46 ms**: **16.20 ms** before the first bank kernel of a token and **24.26 ms** between early-down and late-gate/up kernels, the real-lookup wait. The routed payload **7.333 GB / 55.56 ms = 132 GB/s** effective, against **162–203 GB/s** in the bank-read bench; the original 124 ms was the misclassified sum of both MMVQ families. Short-context `cudaMemcpyAsync` host time (~21 ms/token) is mostly host calls blocking behind GPU work, including the **0.62 MB logits readback/token**, not record copies: no **12 MB expert copies** exist in the trace. Exact attribution of the host API time remains unresolved; it is not an additive savings budget. Long context has host-bank MMVQ **56.87 ms**, device MMVQ **71.05 ms**, and early-to-late gaps **75.20 ms** per captured token. These profiled durations are not an exact partition of unprofiled wall time; kernel-family sums and host API time can overlap GPU work. **Three buckets of similar size: dense-spine GEMV ~68 ms (patch 0027 targets ~7), host-bank expert GEMV ~56 ms at ~132 GB/s (scheduling plan: 10-19 ms ceiling), read waits ~40 ms (hedge/latency work); the 124 ms / 59 GB/s first reading was a classification error, corrected the same night.**

**Host-bank GEMV: the levers, closed.** Scheduling (bg: -60 %), DMA ring (dma: 3x slower), and allocation type (hb: neutral for decode) are closed; huge pages (thp: +1.3 %, the only survivor) help from the fill side. What remains for decode on the Spark is capacity (done), eviction (done), the fill side (THP), and the predictor/read-wait structure (closed: top-k cut neutral, hedge parked). See the [third-pass results](#glm-53-the-candidate-windows-third-pass-2026-10-31-night).

**Batching, first measurement.** The [streaming-decode capacity model](streaming-decode-2026-10-31.md#n-sequences-per-ubatch-explicit-capacity-model-not-a-benchmark) expected about 7.5 aggregate tok/s at N=2 if the dense spine amortises; [window par](evidence/glm-windows-2026-10-30/legs-par.txt) instead measured 4.29, versus 5.52/5.56/5.59 at N=1 (-23 %), and both identical-prompt clients changed bytes, including relative to each other. The source audit in that note explains the lost overlap: `get_split_map` rejects `n_tokens != 1`, so batching leaves the split early/late expert path for ordinary batch fill; the N-column MoE MMVQ kernel accumulates in a different order per column. Today's batching is therefore slower without read/compute overlap, and neither bit-identical nor column-consistent. Window par2 must extend 0008's exact-columns mode to the MoE kernel and show every concurrent stream matches the singleton gold and each other; window ns must then demonstrate 0030's N-column split path with expert-union reads, restored read/compute overlap and an aggregate gain while preserving those bytes. The 7.5 tok/s figure remains a model expectation, not a measured result; ns is measured below; par2 remains required.

**Batching, the N-column split path.** Window ns (16:19) tested 0030 with the 0032 predictor fix on the production x3 composition. [Legs](evidence/glm-windows-2026-10-30/legs-ns.txt) and the [runner log](evidence/glm-windows-2026-10-30/runner33-ns.txt) show N=2 split on restoring read/compute overlap: aggregate throughput rises from 4.30–4.67 on the ordinary batch path to 5.22–5.71 tok/s, back to the N=1 rate, with no aggregate gain yet. The engine proves union reads and one-column MMVQ dispatch: `split columns: N2=28646 N3=0 N4=0 ubatch layers, union=432178 experts, reads=66606 distinct missing records; MMVQ one-column dispatch` — 15.1 distinct experts per layer out of 16 selected, and 2.3 demand reads per layer versus 1.3 at N=1. Initial `split GEMV` summaries report 6.7–7.7 of 8 experts complete at the early lookup; later cumulative multi-column summaries exceed 8, so that counter is not a per-column completion fraction. Each expert is still launched once per column, reading its bytes N times; a 12 MB expert fitting in the 25 MB L2 may partly save consecutive column reads, but cache reuse is not measured. Step time goes from about 0.175 s at N=1 to 0.345 s at N=2, then only 0.38 s at N=4: N=4 is almost free beyond N=2. This suggests a fixed cost switching on at N >= 2, potentially outside the expert path (dense-spine multi-column kernels or batched attention), with extra demand reads also contributing; the [local audit](evidence/glm-windows-2026-10-30/batching-ns-2026-10-31.md) cautions that timing alone does not identify the cause. NS2's `19ee5eaf`/`c592f33e` outputs swap clients between rounds, following column position rather than client identity. Multi-column dense projections, attention and quantization do not guarantee the one-column accumulation order, so the expert-only fix cannot guarantee exact bytes; the audit also calls for checking masks/positions, packing strides and split indexing before attributing the column-position difference solely to dense arithmetic. **N-column split works mechanically (union reads, one hook pair per layer) but is not exact and not faster at N=2; N=4 gives 1.9x aggregate with changed bytes. Next: the nsys breakdown at N=2/4 (window m2) to name the fixed cost, and per-column exact dense kernels (0008 exact columns, window par2) before any production use.** Window par2 (16:44), with exact one-column MMVQ accumulation for both dense and MoE ids, changes the ordinary batch pair to `400f1b3b`/`f451f8a5` but remains column-position dependent and fails gold at 4.19/4.51/4.52 aggregate tok/s; separate launches remove overlap and cost speed, pointing outside MMVQ to batched attention, activation packing or fused norm/RoPE for N columns. **Neither 0008 (exact columns) nor 0030 (N-column split) restores bit identity at N=2; the difference is upstream of the expert path. Next: window m2 (nsys at N=1/2/4) to name the fixed N>=2 cost, and a tensor-level diff at the first layer to find the first differing intermediate.**

**Batching, where the time goes.** Window m2 (17:05) profiles `~/llama-k3-ns` (production x3 composition + 0030/0032 N-column split) with `nsys profile` (`cuda,nvtx,osrt`) around `llama-server --parallel N`, concurrent warmed clients on window m's prompt, and 32 generated tokens per client. The [summary](evidence/glm-windows-2026-10-30/m2/M2-summary.txt), [legs](evidence/glm-windows-2026-10-30/m2/legs-m2.txt) and [runner log](evidence/glm-windows-2026-10-30/m2/runner35-m2.txt) report kernel sums per GENERATED token through `tools/glm53/nsys_breakdown.py`, now separating MMQ/GEMM and attention; prompt work is included, gaps excluded, and kernel sums can overlap (the kernel union is reported separately). The dense spine, attention and the rest amortise with N as the model predicted: dense MMVQ falls 67.7 → 34.9 → 18.8 ms/token at N=1/2/4 split on. The host-bank expert path stays ~53 ms/token, or ~105 ms per two-token step and ~214 ms per four-token step: the split path launches the one-column kernel once per column, streaming each expert's 12 MB again over C2C. This identifies the bank path as the N=2 floor, superseding ns's tentative fixed-cost explanation. The ordinary N-column batch kernel reads the weights once, but at N=2 still takes 40.3 ms/token (~80 ms/step), so it does not amortise either: it is a different, slower kernel, and exposed read waits leave a 187 ms/token wall span despite the lower kernel sum. The lever is 0033 (in design), a true N-column host-bank kernel that streams each distinct expert once and accumulates each column in singleton order. Expected bank time is ~55 ms/step at N=2 and ~60 at N=4; combined with the measured amortised dense/attention/other work (including MMQ/GEMM), this projects roughly 0.12 s/step at N=2 and 0.11 s at N=4, or 16–36 aggregate tok/s **if the reads keep up** (2.3 demand reads per layer at N=2 versus 1.3 at N=1). These are projections, not measured throughput. **At N>1 everything amortises except the host-bank expert GEMV, which is launched per column and re-streams the weights; an N-column exact bank kernel (one stream per distinct expert, per-column singleton accumulation order) is the lever for both speed and the bytes. Exactness upstream (attention/packing) is still open: the first-differing-tensor tool is in design.**

**Batching, the ceiling.** Window par8 (17:41) tests the production x3 binary on the ordinary batch path (no N-column split), cache 84000 MiB, `--ctx-size 65536 --parallel N`, with concurrent clients on the standard 128-token prompt at temperature 0 and three measured rounds after a warm round; md5 gold is enforced only at N=1. The [legs-par8.txt](evidence/glm-windows-2026-10-30/legs-par8.txt), [runner37-par8.txt](evidence/glm-windows-2026-10-30/runner37-par8.txt) evidence puts N=8 at 17.07–19.57 aggregate tok/s (about 3.3x the single-sequence rate), with roughly 2.3 tok/s per stream, right at the ~19 tok/s ceiling implied by host-bank C2C bandwidth when the bank path is linear in N (m2). N=16 falls to 12.30–12.87 aggregate tok/s. The window prints the c-tree source dispatch expectation: N=8 keeps experts on the MMVQ MoE kernel (cap 8) and Q2_K dense on MMQ; N=16 moves experts to MMQ too. This is not verified binary dispatch, and partial/draining batches may use MMVQ again. The interpretation is that the kernel transition and demand reads per step doubling again explain the fall. The ubatches > 8 cache summaries include ~72.8 % hits and ~42,000 MiB uploaded (roughly 42 GB) in each N=1 control; these are not a whole-window total or the N=16 rate, whose leg reports 76.56 % hits. The box can therefore serve ~18 tokens per second to eight concurrent users today, at roughly 2.3 tok/s each and with bytes that differ from the N=1 gold. **Throughput ceiling of plain batching on the Spark: ~18-19.6 aggregate tok/s at N=8 (3.3x), falling at N=16. Not usable under the bit-identical rule until the N>1 divergence is found (window fd, tool 0034); if the owner accepts non-identical bytes for multi-user serving, `--parallel 8` is the setting.**

**Batching, where the bytes diverge.** Window fd (17:36 first attempt / 18:12 rerun) instruments `~/llama-k3-par2` (production x3 + 0008 exact MMVQ columns) with 0034 v2: a scheduler eval observer hashes every compute node per column with FNV-1a over the column's bytes, using an 8 MiB streaming buffer and skipping weights and tensors over 64 MiB, for two decode graphs after the prompt. N=1 is compared with N=2 (two clients, the same standard prompt, temperature 0); `tools/glm53/tensor_firstdiff.py` aligns nodes by step, name and order. The first attempt's v1 observer fetched multi-GB tensors and OOM-killed the server (memory valve released 42 GB, MemAvailable 0), before it fetched weight-backed views; this is the supplied run account, since `legs-fd-v1-oom.txt` is absent from the supplied evidence directory. The [v2 legs](evidence/glm-windows-2026-10-30/fd/legs-fd-v2.txt) and [runner38 log](evidence/glm-windows-2026-10-30/fd/runner38-fd.txt) accompany the four per-node digest files ([FD10.tsv](evidence/glm-windows-2026-10-30/fd/FD10.tsv), [FD20.tsv](evidence/glm-windows-2026-10-30/fd/FD20.tsv), [FD11.tsv](evidence/glm-windows-2026-10-30/fd/FD11.tsv), [FD21.tsv](evidence/glm-windows-2026-10-30/fd/FD21.tsv)): FD10/FD20 are N=1/2, FD11/FD21 repeat with `GGML_CUDA_MMVQ_EXACT_COLS=1`. V2 collected 20807 lines at N=1 and 29236 at N=2 without the observer OOM; the runner nevertheless flags missing/duplicate step-0 column-1 logits for both N=2 legs, so its full-leg validation did not pass. Column 0, the first client, samples 481, 468, 952 in both N=1 and N=2. The supplied comparator account reports only 6 matches among 7056 aligned nodes at decode step 1: the first six nodes are `embd` GET_ROWS MATCH, `node_1` GET_ROWS DIFF, `node_2` DUP DIFF, `SET_ROWS (view)` DIFF, `norm-0` RMS_NORM MATCH, `attn_norm-0` MUL MATCH; then `indexer_k-0` MUL_MAT (f32, 128 rows, the DSA indexer K projection) DIFF, with downstream divergence. The same first compute divergence appears with exact MMVQ columns on (FD11 vs FD21), so 0008 does not touch it. The matching norm after the slot-dependent `node_1`/`node_2`/`SET_ROWS` bookkeeping shows identical per-token activations through the attention norm. The interpretation is that this small non-MoE projection takes a different two-column kernel (float path or MMQ) from the one-column kernel; the DSA indexer selects attended keys from these values, propagating the difference downstream. The fix path is 0035 (in Sol design): dispatch every 2..8-column MUL_MAT as one-column launches under a knob, then look for the next divergence in attention over two sequences or the MoE merge. **First N-dependent op found: the DSA indexer K projection (`indexer_k-0` MUL_MAT) at decode step 1; activations are bit-identical up to the attention norm. Exactness at N>1 is a matmul-dispatch problem, not a batching-semantics problem; 0035 (exact float/quant columns for every MUL_MAT) is the next window.** Window fd3 (18:24 first launch / 18:26 rerun) adds 0035 to `~/llama-k3-par2` (production x3 + 0008 + 0034 v2): under `GGML_CUDA_EXACT_COLS=1`, every MUL_MAT with `ne[1]` in 2..8 dispatches as one-column launches. FD30 is N=1 and FD31 is N=2, with `GGML_CUDA_EXACT_COLS=1 GGML_CUDA_MMVQ_EXACT_COLS=1`; two clients use the same standard prompt at temperature 0, with three sampled tokens per client (481, 468, 952 in both) aligned by the comparator. Evidence: [legs-fd-v3.txt](evidence/glm-windows-2026-10-30/fd3/legs-fd-v3.txt), [runner40-fd3.txt](evidence/glm-windows-2026-10-30/fd3/runner40-fd3.txt), [runner39-fd3-buildfail.txt](evidence/glm-windows-2026-10-30/fd3/runner39-fd3-buildfail.txt), [FD30.tsv](evidence/glm-windows-2026-10-30/fd3/FD30.tsv), [FD31.tsv](evidence/glm-windows-2026-10-30/fd3/FD31.tsv). The first launch failed to compile because 0035 used `ggml_cuda_exact_cols` before its definition; a forward declaration fixed it. The indexer K projection now matches at N=2: the three ROPE/MUL_MAT/VIEW predecessors of `indexer_k-0` match for both columns. The window comparator then stopped at a structural KV-cache write difference (`SET_ROWS` view at N=1 vs `RESHAPE` at N=2), which is bookkeeping. Supplied hand alignment of compute nodes over the first four layers for column 0 (first client) finds 134/150 matches, versus 45/150 without 0035. Remaining differences are `indexer_q-L` MUL_MAT, whose token batch is `ne[2]` (shape `(128,32,N)`) and is not split by 0035; `indexer_score-L` LIGHTNING_INDEXER downstream of it; and the layer 3 MoE experts (`ffn_moe_gate/up/swiglu/down-3`): `(2048,9,1)` at N=1 uses the split early/late path with the 0009 dummy row, while `(2048,8,2)` at N=2 uses the ordinary ids path, with kernels differing from the N=1 reference even under per-column singleton dispatch. Column 1 (second client) differs almost everywhere because its prompt was prefilled in a mixed ubatch (127 prompt tokens, then the last prompt token with client A's decode token); prefill batching is a separate exactness problem. This leaves three concrete N-dependent decode sites (the `ne[2]` projection, its DSA indexer score op, and the MoE route), plus a fourth for prefill. Next is fd4 (in Sol design), combining 0030's N-column split path so N=2 uses the same expert kernels and dummy-row structure as N=1, with 0035 v2 splitting whichever axis carries the tokens. **0035 closes the first divergence; 134/150 compute nodes of the first four layers now match at N=2. Remaining: ne[2]-batched indexer_q, LIGHTNING_INDEXER, and the MoE route (needs the 0030 split path at N=2); prefill in mixed ubatches diverges separately.**

**Batching, bit-identical decode at N=2.** Window fd4 (18:36) tests combined exactness in `~/llama-k3-fd4`: the ns tree (production x3 + 0030 v2 N-column split + 0032), plus 0008 exact MMVQ columns, 0034 v2 digest observer, 0035 v2 (one-column dispatch for every MUL_MAT batched in `ne[1]` or `ne[2]`, 2..8 tokens), and 0036 (per-query singleton flash-attention ops and argsort-based DSA selection). Both legs set `GGML_CUDA_EXACT_COLS=1 GGML_CUDA_MMVQ_EXACT_COLS=1 LLAMA_EXACT_ATTN_COLS=1 GGML_CUDA_ARGSORT_EXACT_ROWS=1 GGML_CUDA_DISABLE_FUSION=1`; N=2 also sets `LLAMA_EXPERT_STORE_SPLIT_COLS=1`. Two clients use the standard prompt, with three sampled tokens and three decode graphs digested; FD40 is N=1 and FD41 is N=2. Evidence: [legs-fd4.txt](evidence/glm-windows-2026-10-30/fd4/legs-fd4.txt), [runner41-fd4.txt](evidence/glm-windows-2026-10-30/fd4/runner41-fd4.txt), [FD40.tsv](evidence/glm-windows-2026-10-30/fd4/FD40.tsv), [FD41.tsv](evidence/glm-windows-2026-10-30/fd4/FD41.tsv). For column 0 (the first client, whose prompt was prefilled alone), the N=2 logits digests equal N=1 at steps 0/1/2: `f7b741495679b61a`, `4cb7733844ea31fe`, `57c90f78fbdb3199`. All 77 residual `l_out-L` tensors match, `ffn_moe_out-L` matches in every layer, and dense layers 0–2 match 100 %. At step 1, the comparator's remaining 473 differences among 4303 aligned nodes are expert-store bookkeeping GET_ROWS (`moe_prefetch`, `moe_early`, `ffn_moe_slots`, `moe_nowait_merge`) and, in some layers, 9-row MUL_MAT_ID/GLU outputs whose dummy/scratch rows hold run-dependent bytes the merge never reads. Column 1 (the second client) has only 324/4303 matches because its prompt was prefilled in a mixed ubatch; prefill isolation (0037) is in design. The exact-mode N=1 logits (`f7b741...`) differ from the production single-sequence path (FD10/FD30: `d6bf1d66...`): one of the exact flags changes the single-sequence bytes, with fusion off the suspect. Whether the 128-token md5 still equals gold `9449ae5738` is the next window, fdg; exact-mode cost at N=2 was not measured. **Decode at N=2 is now bit-identical per sequence to the single-sequence run under the exact flags (0030 split + 0008 + 0035 v2 + 0036): logits and every residual match for the client whose prompt was prefilled alone. Open: the exact-mode N=1 bytes vs the production gold (window fdg), prefill isolation for the other client (0037), and the cost of the exact mode at N=2 (not measured).**

**Batching, the exact mode against the gold.** Window fdg (18:48) checks the exact mode against the gold using the fd4 binary `~/llama-k3-fd4` (production x3 + 0030 v2/0032 N-column split + 0008 + 0034 v2 + 0035 v2 + 0036), cache 84000 MiB, the standard 128-token request (`temperature=0`, `seed=1`, `n_predict=128`), and a fresh server per leg. N=1: G0 has no flags; G1 sets `GGML_CUDA_EXACT_COLS=1 GGML_CUDA_MMVQ_EXACT_COLS=1`; G2 adds `LLAMA_EXACT_ATTN_COLS=1`; G3 adds `GGML_CUDA_ARGSORT_EXACT_ROWS=1`; G4 adds `GGML_CUDA_DISABLE_FUSION=1`. G5 is N=2 with all G4 flags plus `LLAMA_EXPERT_STORE_SPLIT_COLS=1`, client A first and B 3 s later, three rounds. [legs-fdg.txt](evidence/glm-windows-2026-10-30/fdg/legs-fdg.txt) and [runner43-fdg.txt](evidence/glm-windows-2026-10-30/fdg/runner43-fdg.txt): G0 is GOLD, md5 `9449ae5738`, at 5.23 tok/s, confirming the patches are inert without flags. G1–G4 are all NOT-GOLD, md5 `400f1b3bb1`, at 4.50/4.43/4.40/4.47 tok/s. The bytes move away from gold already at G1: 0035 v2 turns CUDA op fusion and pinned memory off whenever the exact flag is set; the attention/argsort/fusion flags add no further change. G5 client A is `400f1b3bb1` in all three rounds (A-vs-G4 MATCH), full 128-token identity with exact-mode N=1; client B is stably `03bdde8265`, different because its prompt was prefilled in a mixed ubatch. Aggregate throughput is 5.21/5.19/5.17 tok/s, about 2.78 per stream. `400f1b3bb1` is also the md5 par2 saw for the N=2 exact-columns client: that run already matched exact-mode N=1, but the exact-mode N=1 reference was missing then. The exact mode is self-consistent end to end for the client prefilled alone; the only gap to production gold is fusion. Exact mode must keep the fused kernels used by the N=1 production path (0035 v3 in design). Today it costs about 14 % at N=1 with fusion and pinned memory off; N=2 exact gives about 5.2 aggregate, no gain over N=1 yet, and ns showed the split path itself is bank-bound. **Exact mode is bit-identical end to end at N=2 for a lone-prefilled client (A == exact N=1 in all rounds) and the patches are inert when off; it is NOT the production gold because 0035 v2 turns fusion off (G1 already moves the bytes). Next: fusion-preserving exact mode (0035 v3, 0036 v2), prefill isolation for the second client (0037, window fd5), then the cost at N=2..8.**

**Batching, prefill isolation.** Window fd5 (19:06) tests prefill isolation (0037) in `~/llama-k3-fd5`: the fd4 tree (production x3 + 0030 v2/0032 + 0008 + 0034 v2 + 0035 v2 + 0036) plus 0037. Under `LLAMA_PREFILL_ISOLATE=1`, a pending prompt owns the batch: no decode tokens or other prompts mix into its ubatches, and its ubatch boundaries are standalone; decode batching resumes afterwards. Both legs set `GGML_CUDA_EXACT_COLS=1 GGML_CUDA_MMVQ_EXACT_COLS=1 LLAMA_EXACT_ATTN_COLS=1 GGML_CUDA_ARGSORT_EXACT_ROWS=1 GGML_CUDA_DISABLE_FUSION=1`; N=2 additionally sets `LLAMA_EXPERT_STORE_SPLIT_COLS=1` and `LLAMA_PREFILL_ISOLATE=1`. FD50 is N=1; FD51 is N=2 with two concurrent clients on the standard prompt, three sampled tokens, and digests of the prompt logits and two decode graphs. Evidence: [legs-fd5.txt](evidence/glm-windows-2026-10-30/fd5/legs-fd5.txt), [runner44-fd5.txt](evidence/glm-windows-2026-10-30/fd5/runner44-fd5.txt), [FD50.tsv](evidence/glm-windows-2026-10-30/fd5/FD50.tsv), [FD51.tsv](evidence/glm-windows-2026-10-30/fd5/FD51.tsv). For both clients, the prefill logits and sampled token equal N=1 (the window's PREFILL check: 2/2 output records match per column). The logits digests at steps 0/1/2 are `f7b741495679b61a`, `4cb7733844ea31fe`, `57c90f78fbdb3199` at N=1 and in each N=2 column; the tokens are 481, 468, 952 throughout. The window's decode summary, `14/23204 observed decode records match`, is a defect of the new `prefill_compare.py` alignment, which compares raw records across differently shaped graphs, not an engine failure. The per-logits comparison here and the fd4 per-node comparison method are the valid comparisons. Isolation serialises a prompt (~1.2 s for 128 tokens at 109 tok/s) while other streams wait. Prefill isolation plus the exact mode makes every sequence in the N=2 batch reproduce the single-sequence bytes; the mode is functionally complete for decode and prefill. The remaining production-gold gap is fusion: fdg2 with 0035 v3 / 0036 v2 is next, followed by the mode's cost at N=2..8 in fde. **With 0037 both clients at N=2 are bit-identical to the single-sequence run (prefill and decode). The exact batching mode is functionally complete; the gold gap is fusion only (fdg2 next), and its throughput is unmeasured (fde).**

**Batching, fusion preserved.** Window fdg2 (19:15) checks fusion-preserving exact mode against the gold in `~/llama-k3-fdg2`: the fd4 tree with 0035 v2 and 0036 replaced by 0035 v3 and 0036 v2, incrementally built. 0035 v3 keeps production fusion and pinned-memory policy: each exact column launches the original fused bias/GLU epilogue, and broadcast `ne[2]` token batches are sliced. 0036 v2 runs N original singleton TOP_K ops for N>1 multi-sequence decode instead of ARGSORT; N=1 is untouched. See the [fusion inventory](fusion-at-N-2026-10-31.md). The standard 128-token request (`temperature=0`, `seed=1`, `n_predict=128`) uses a fresh server per leg and cache 84000 MiB. G0 has no flags; G1 sets `GGML_CUDA_EXACT_COLS=1 GGML_CUDA_MMVQ_EXACT_COLS=1`; G2 adds `LLAMA_EXACT_ATTN_COLS=1`; G3 adds `GGML_CUDA_ARGSORT_EXACT_ROWS=1`; G4 repeats G3. No leg sets `GGML_CUDA_DISABLE_FUSION`. G5 is N=2 with the G4 flags plus `LLAMA_EXPERT_STORE_SPLIT_COLS=1`, client A first and B 3 s later, three rounds; this tree has no prefill isolation. Evidence: [legs-fdg2.txt](evidence/glm-windows-2026-10-30/fdg2/legs-fdg2.txt) and [runner45-fdg2.txt](evidence/glm-windows-2026-10-30/fdg2/runner45-fdg2.txt). G0–G4 are all GOLD, byte-identical to production (md5 `9449ae5738f2e56f99ab7ab6e9626eb5`), at 5.26/5.27/5.25/5.18/5.24 tok/s respectively: exact mode costs nothing at N=1, versus about 14 % for v2 with fusion off. G5 client A has md5 `2e8fdf8f3be21093` in all three rounds: stable, but neither gold nor v2 exact bytes. Client B has md5 `b5933beb8819fbf0`, the ordinary batch path's second-column bytes from windows par, NS2off and par8. Aggregate throughput is 5.17/5.21/5.18 tok/s, about 2.76 per stream. The runner reports `FDG2-ACCEPT FAIL` for both clients in every N=2 round. The fusion-preserving rewrite closes the N=1 gold gap but regresses N=2: B equalling plain batching indicates that at least part of the graph no longer takes per-column exact dispatch, while A's residual differences come from whatever still splits. The fd6 digest window on the fde tree, with 0035 v4 / 0036 v3 in design, will identify the site. **Fusion-preserving exact mode is gold and free at N=1 (five legs, 5.18-5.27 tok/s). At N=2 it regressed: the per-column dispatch no longer engages everywhere (B equals plain batching). Next: fd6 digest on the fde tree with the v4 fix.**

**Batching, the exact mode's throughput.** Window fde (19:36) measures exact-mode throughput in `~/llama-k3-fde`: the fdg2 tree (production x3 + 0030 v2/0032 + 0008 + 0034 v2 + 0035 v3 + 0036 v2) plus 0037 prefill isolation, incrementally built. Each leg has a fresh server, cache 84000 MiB, context 65536 and `--parallel N`; concurrent clients start together on the standard 128-token request, with a warm round then three measured rounds and a 300 s server cap. E1 is N=1 with no flags; E1x is N=1 with `GGML_CUDA_EXACT_COLS=1 GGML_CUDA_MMVQ_EXACT_COLS=1 LLAMA_EXACT_ATTN_COLS=1 GGML_CUDA_ARGSORT_EXACT_ROWS=1`. E2/E4/E8 use the same flags at N=2/4/8 plus `LLAMA_EXPERT_STORE_SPLIT_COLS=1 LLAMA_PREFILL_ISOLATE=1`; E1b repeats the unflagged control. Evidence: [legs-fde.txt](evidence/glm-windows-2026-10-30/fde/legs-fde.txt) and [runner46-fde.txt](evidence/glm-windows-2026-10-30/fde/runner46-fde.txt). E1 gives 5.45/5.54/5.55 aggregate tok/s (5.72/5.82/5.83 per-stream mean), gold 1/1; E1x gives 5.47/5.57/5.57 (5.75/5.85/5.87), gold 1/1: the exact flags cost nothing at N=1. E2 gives 6.97/6.95/6.96 aggregate (3.63 per stream), gold 0/2, but both clients have identical bytes in every round (md5 `232e8f4e`): column-position dependence is gone. These are the ordinary-batch first-column bytes, the v3 regression indicating part of the graph is still on batch kernels. E4 gives 8.05/7.99 aggregate (2.07 per stream), gold 0/4, all four clients with identical bytes; round 3 is cut by the 300 s cap. E8 hits the cap before a measured round; 0030's split path supports only N <= 4, so N=8 experts fall back to the ordinary MoE path (draining batches of at most four can split). E1b gives 5.44/5.53/5.54 aggregate, gold 1/1. With prefill isolation and per-column paths, exact mode gains +26 % aggregate at N=2 and +45 % at N=4 over single-stream, versus about 5.2 tok/s at N=2 for v2 exact mode and 4.3–4.7 for plain batching at N=2; every client in a batch gets the same deterministic bytes. The N>1 gold gap is the v3 regression: joint routed/shared MoE fusion bypassed the per-column dispatcher, fixed in 0035 v4 and being verified by the fd6 digest window. N=8 needs the split path extended beyond four columns and a longer server cap. **Exact mode: free at N=1 (gold), +26 % at N=2 and +45 % at N=4 aggregate with deterministic, client-independent bytes; not yet gold at N>1 (v3 regression, v4 under test). The practical ceiling of plain batching remains 17-19.6 at N=8 with non-gold bytes.**

**Batching, the observer effect.** Window fd6 (20:05) tests 0035 v4 digest and gold in `~/llama-k3-fd6`: the fde tree with 0035 v3 replaced by v4, fully rebuilt. V4 slices the joint routed/shared MoE fusion per column BEFORE the input conversion, so the per-column dispatch covers it. FD60 is N=1 with the digest observer on and three sampled tokens; FD61 is N=2 with the observer on and all exact flags (`GGML_CUDA_EXACT_COLS=1 GGML_CUDA_MMVQ_EXACT_COLS=1 LLAMA_EXACT_ATTN_COLS=1 GGML_CUDA_ARGSORT_EXACT_ROWS=1`) plus `LLAMA_EXPERT_STORE_SPLIT_COLS=1 LLAMA_PREFILL_ISOLATE=1`. FD6G1 is N=1 with all flags and a 128-token request without the observer; FD6G2 uses the same flags at N=2 with two clients, also without the observer. Evidence: [legs-fd6.txt](evidence/glm-windows-2026-10-30/fd6/legs-fd6.txt) and [runner47-fd6.txt](evidence/glm-windows-2026-10-30/fd6/runner47-fd6.txt). With the observer on, both columns PASS: prefill logits and sampled tokens equal N=1 (2/2 output records per column), and neither column has a compute divergence in two decode graphs. The fixed comparator excludes bookkeeping and expected scratch rows; those scratch differences are not exact matches. Without the observer, FD6G1 is GOLD (`9449ae5738`), but both FD6G2 clients are NOT-GOLD (`232e8f4e2fb04d3b`), exactly the plain-batching first-column md5 from par, NS2off and par8: in the full run the exact paths did not take effect at all. The interpretation is an observer effect: the digest observer's eval callback disables CUDA graph capture and splits the backend graph, allowing the per-column exact dispatch to run. Without it, decode is replayed as a CUDA graph and bypasses the per-column paths: host synchronisation is not possible inside a captured region, so dispatch falls back to batched kernels. This explains why v2 exact mode, whose side effects also kept graphs off, matched end to end, while v3/v4 match only under the observer. The fde throughput figures therefore do not establish the cost of an exact full run at N>1. **v4 is exact at N=2 when the observer is on (both columns PASS) but the full run without the observer produces plain-batching bytes: the per-column paths are bypassed under CUDA graph replay. Next: make the exact dispatch capture-safe or disable CUDA graphs in exact mode and measure the cost (window fdg3).** Window fdg3 (20:43) rules out that CUDA-graph explanation: both N=2 clients produce identical plain-batching bytes (`232e8f4e`) with graphs on and off, while N=1 remains gold either way ([legs-fdg3.txt](evidence/glm-windows-2026-10-30/fdg3/legs-fdg3.txt), [runner49-fdg3.txt](evidence/glm-windows-2026-10-30/fdg3/runner49-fdg3.txt)). The remaining explanation is the short-context KV extent / DSA selection identified in fdw (0036 v3/v4), possibly with fused-path dispatch (0035 v5); fd8/fd9 test these next.

**Batching, where the short prompt diverges.** Window fdw (20:18) tests warm-cache and unequal-prompt gates on the fd6 tree (production x3 + 0030 v2/0032 + 0008 + 0034 v2 + 0035 v4 + 0036 v2 + 0037), with no observer, a fresh server per leg, a 600 s cap, cache 84000 MiB and context 65536. All legs set all six exact knobs: `GGML_CUDA_EXACT_COLS=1 GGML_CUDA_MMVQ_EXACT_COLS=1 LLAMA_EXACT_ATTN_COLS=1 GGML_CUDA_ARGSORT_EXACT_ROWS=1 LLAMA_EXPERT_STORE_SPLIT_COLS=1 LLAMA_PREFILL_ISOLATE=1`. The standard request is the 10-token quicksort prompt with 128 generated tokens (`n_predict=128`); earlier rows' “standard 128-token request” refers to the generated length, not the prompt length. The long request uses a deterministic 2048-token prompt and 128 generated tokens. Warm means `cache_prompt=true`, measured on the second request against the corresponding standalone warm reference. Evidence: [legs-fdw.txt](evidence/glm-windows-2026-10-30/fdw/legs-fdw.txt) and [runner48-fdw.txt](evidence/glm-windows-2026-10-30/fdw/runner48-fdw.txt). W1 establishes standalone N=1 references: standard cold is gold (`9449ae5738`) at 5.17 tok/s, standard warm is `f675805e` at 4.36; long cold is `2f26e153`, with 40.6 s prefill (~50 tok/s) and 5.58 tok/s decode, and long warm retains `2f26e153` at 6.37 tok/s. W2 starts standard + long cold together at N=2: long REF-MATCH, standard REF-MISMATCH (`232e8f4e`), 2.80 aggregate tok/s. W3 measures both warm: long REF-MATCH, standard REF-MISMATCH (`077218a3`), 5.47 aggregate tok/s. W4 starts long cold, then standard 2 s later: long REF-MATCH, standard REF-MISMATCH (`232e8f4e`), 2.74 aggregate tok/s. W5 starts two standard + two long cold at N=4: both long clients REF-MATCH (`2f26e153`), both standard clients REF-MISMATCH (`232e8f4e`), 3.43 aggregate tok/s. In every batched configuration the 2048-token client reproduces its standalone bytes, cold and warm, while the 10-token client never does. This narrows the earlier observer-effect interpretation: the remaining divergence follows short context. The short client's context stays under 256 valid keys, below the DSA indexer's `top_k=2048`, so selection must take all valid keys. What changes between standalone and batched execution is the KV extent seen by selection and attention: 0036 v2 uses the common padded extent across streams, giving the short client K = 2048 instead of its standalone K = 256. The earlier padding tie-order explanation is superseded by window fag below: selected membership is identical in every differing case, while the larger effective KV changes attention rounding. A client with >= 2048 valid keys has the same K in both cases. The fix is per-stream standalone extents and dispatch (0036 v4); fd9 measures it. Exact mode also slows the 2048-token prefill to ~50 tok/s from ~109; the cause is under investigation. **Warm cache and unequal prompts are exact for the long client in every batch shape; the remaining divergence is the short-context regime (valid keys < top_k) where the batched effective KV extent changes attention rounding despite identical selected membership (proven by fag). Fix: per-stream standalone extents and dispatch (0036 v4); fd9 measures it. Prefill is 2x slower under the exact flags: must be fixed before any trial.**

**Batching, the attention geometry proof.** Window fag (21:01), the flash-attention geometry probe, runs a standalone CUDA program linked against the production tree's ggml (`~/llama-k3-fd6/build-fd4`), beside the fd8 build, with no model or server. The [probe source](../tools/glm53/probe/fattn-geometry.cu) builds GLM DSA decode attention for ONE query token exactly as `glm-dsa.cpp` does: Q 576x1x64 heads, one F16 KV head, V aliasing K's first 512 channels, scale 1/16, production TOP_K/CONT and SET_ROWS selection, mask add, and F32 accumulation. Fixed pseudo-random Q/K/V are tested with KV extent E in {256, 512, 2048, 4096, 32768, 65536}, n_valid finite keys in {10, 138, 300} (the rest -INF), and K either the production rule min(E, 2048) or the standalone rule (n_valid padded to 256). Each of 34 configurations is compared bit for bit against E equal to the standalone extent, with identical selected membership verified. The [evidence](evidence/glm-windows-2026-10-30/fag/legs-fag.txt) reports `SUMMARY configs=34 differing=12 membership=identical repeats=identical verdict=GEOMETRY_SENSITIVE`. For valid=138, E=2048/4096/32768/65536 with K=2048 differ from the E=256 baseline by max_abs ~1.6e-4; K=256 restores bit identity on the sparse path, but dense dispatch forces effective KV=E at E=512 and 2048, so lowering K there does not restore it. For valid=300, K=2048 differs from the E=512 baseline by ~9.2e-5, while K=512 is identical under the sparse path; dense E=2048 again forces effective KV=E. All valid=10 configurations match. With the same valid keys and selected set, changing effective KV can therefore change the FA output. This identifies the mechanism behind the short-prompt divergence at N>1: a batched ubatch gives the short stream the common extent's K=2048 instead of its standalone K=256, changing the kernel's partition/reduction over effective KV and its rounding; the long client (>= 2048 valid keys) keeps the same K and matches. The fix must give each stream its standalone extent and dispatch (0036 v4, window fd9). The padding tie-order explanation in fdw is superseded: membership was identical in every differing case. **Proven: the DSA decode attention output depends on the effective KV extent even with identical selected keys (12 of 34 configurations differ, membership identical). Per-stream standalone extents (0036 v4) are the required fix; fd9 measures it.**

**Batching, bit-identical for every client.** Window fd8 (21:00) tests per-sequence KV extent, 0036 v3, in `~/llama-k3-fd8`: the fd6 tree with 0036 v2 replaced by v3; 0038 did not apply (preimage differs) and was absent. Exact DSA attention uses one sequence per graph: each stream's own score, TOP_K with K = min(2048, its own padded extent), mask and flash-attention (FA) geometry, reproducing its standalone geometry. This version serialises the sequences, so it answers the bytes question, not throughput. All six knobs are set: `GGML_CUDA_EXACT_COLS=1 GGML_CUDA_MMVQ_EXACT_COLS=1 LLAMA_EXACT_ATTN_COLS=1 GGML_CUDA_ARGSORT_EXACT_ROWS=1 LLAMA_EXPERT_STORE_SPLIT_COLS=1 LLAMA_PREFILL_ISOLATE=1`; no observer, fresh server per leg, 600 s cap. The standard request has a 10-token prompt + 128 generated tokens; the long request has a 2048-token prompt + 128 generated tokens. Both run cold and warm (`cache_prompt=true` after a priming request). Evidence: [legs-fd8.txt](evidence/glm-windows-2026-10-30/fd8/legs-fd8.txt) and [runner50-fd8.txt](evidence/glm-windows-2026-10-30/fd8/runner50-fd8.txt). F1 (N=1 standard): cold gold `9449ae5738` at 5.21 tok/s, warm `f675805e` at 4.93. L1 (N=1 long): cold `5e7a436f`, prefill 34.0 s (~60 tok/s), decode 5.96 tok/s; warm 6.73. F2 (N=2 standard + standard): cold both GOLD at 2.72 / 2.74 tok/s per stream; warm both `f675805e`, REF-MATCH. F2m (N=2 standard + long): cold both REF-MATCH at 2.34 / 2.29, warm both REF-MATCH at 2.38 / 2.37 tok/s. L2 (N=2 long + long): cold both REF-MATCH at 1.69 / 3.08, warm both REF-MATCH at 3.25 / 3.27 tok/s. Every client in every configuration reproduced its standalone bytes. With per-sequence standalone attention geometry, exact mode is complete for short and long prompts, cold and warm, in equal and unequal batches. The serialised form gives no throughput gain: about one single-sequence rate split across clients. The non-serialising form (0036 v4: per-stream extents inside one graph) is window fd9, not yet run. Long-prompt prefill is still ~60 tok/s under the flags versus ~109 in production. **Every client in every batch shape is now bit-identical to its standalone run (short/long, cold/warm, equal/unequal), with per-sequence standalone attention geometry. Serialised form: no throughput gain. fd9 (one graph, per-stream extents) decides whether exact batching pays.**

**Batching, the joint-graph attempt.** Window fd9 (00:03) tests `~/llama-k3-fd9`, the fd8 tree with 0036 v3 replaced by v4 (one graph per ubatch, each stream's own padded extent for DSA score/TOP_K/mask/FA), 0035 v4 replaced by v5 (per-column launches inside all fused matvec sites), 0030 v3 (split path through N<=8), 0039 (N=1 inertness guard and unified-KV rejection), and 0038 present. All six knobs from fd8 are set, with no observer, a 600 s cap, and the window stopping at the first failed gate; standard (10-token) and long (2048-token) prompts are tested cold/prime/warm. Evidence: [legs-fd9.txt](evidence/glm-windows-2026-10-30/fd9/legs-fd9.txt), [runner52-fd9.txt](evidence/glm-windows-2026-10-30/fd9/runner52-fd9.txt) and [first-launch gate-bug log](evidence/glm-windows-2026-10-30/fd9/legs-fd9-run1-gatebug.txt). The first launch stopped on a comparator bug: the prime request was gated against the cold reference although the server already held the prompt; engine output was gold in every leg of that run too. N=1 standard cold is gold `9449ae5738` at 4.89 aggregate tok/s (5.2 decode), prime/warm `f675805e`; long cold and prime/warm are `5e7a436f`, prime/warm at 6.7 tok/s. The no-flag P0s/P0 controls have identical bytes. PREFILL ratio 0.989 (limit 1.05) shows the exact flags do not slow prefill: the earlier 2x gap (50–60 versus 109 tok/s) was the windows' ubatch 512 versus production's 2048. N=2 standard + standard fails for client 0: cold `232e8f4e` (plain-batching bytes), prime/warm `74d484f8` (not the warm reference). GATE-FAIL stops the window; no N=2 throughput number is reported. The failed real-run gate left the joint-graph geometry and dispatch unresolved; fd10 below separates them.

Window fd10 (00:29) compares the fd8 tree (0036 v3, one graph per sequence) with the fd9 tree (0036 v4, per-stream extents in one graph; 0035 v5; 0039). The digest observer is on for three tokens in the N=1 and N=2 standard + standard legs, with `LLAMA_PREFILL_ISOLATE=1`; FA source shapes are recorded per column. Then 0036 v5 (canonical per-stream KV strides and `v_mla` per stream) is applied and built on the fd9 tree for a real N=2 128-token gold leg without the observer. Evidence: [legs-fd10.txt](evidence/glm-windows-2026-10-30/fd10/legs-fd10.txt), [runner54-fd10.txt](evidence/glm-windows-2026-10-30/fd10/runner54-fd10.txt) and [fd10-v5-crash.txt](evidence/glm-windows-2026-10-30/fd10/fd10-v5-crash.txt). V4 N=2 versus N=1 has no compute divergence in either column (both PASS) under the observer; each column's shapes equal N=1's: `indexer_score` 256, `SET_ROWS` 1x256, `FLASH_ATTN_EXT` 512x64. The v3 control comparison reports divergences at `norm-10` / `norm-4`, an alignment artifact of its one-graph-per-sequence layout: the comparator numbers steps per graph. These are not evidence of a real v3 compute divergence. The v5 build passes, but the real leg dies at `ggml.c:1825 GGML_ASSERT(obj_new)`: the graph context runs out of node slots because v5's per-stream packing exceeds llama's max-nodes budget. There is no gold or throughput number. V4's geometry is right under the observer (no fusion), yet the real fused N=2 run in fd9 gave plain-batching bytes. Together with v2 (fusion off) being self-consistent and v3's per-sequence graphs being gold, this supports the reading that per-column exact dispatch does not engage in real fused runs: fused ops bypass the splitter, and N=1 stays gold because it needs no splitting. The v5 fused-site split is the candidate fix, but it crashed on the node budget. Next is a node-budget fix plus exact-dispatch counters printed by the engine, so a real run proves which path ran (fd11). **Under the observer v4 is exact and its attention geometry equals standalone; in real fused runs the per-column dispatch is not engaging. The fused-site split (0035/0036 v5) crashed on the graph node budget. Next: node budget + exact-dispatch counters (fd11). The only version gold at N>1 in real runs remains the per-sequence-graph v3, which does not batch.**

**Batching, the fused-kernel culprit.** Window fd11 (00:50) tests the fd9 tree with 0035 v6 (per-column fused-site launches plus `GGML_CUDA_EXACT_STATS=1` exit counters per dispatch class), 0036 v5, 0039, and 0040 (graph max-nodes raised under exact mode, fixing fd10's `GGML_ASSERT(obj_new)`). All exact flags and `LLAMA_PREFILL_ISOLATE=1` are set; N=2 also uses `LLAMA_EXPERT_STORE_SPLIT_COLS=1`. The standard request (10-token prompt + 128 generated tokens) runs cold/prime/warm in three legs: N=1, N=2 fusion on, and N=2 with `GGML_CUDA_DISABLE_FUSION=1`, without the observer. Evidence: [legs-fd11.txt](evidence/glm-windows-2026-10-30/fd11/legs-fd11.txt) and [runner56-fd11.txt](evidence/glm-windows-2026-10-30/fd11/runner56-fd11.txt). A first launch at 00:48 aborted on a missing staged evidence path; no engine run occurred. N=1 is gold (`9449ae5738` cold, `f675805e` prime/warm), at 4.95 tok/s warm aggregate. N=2 fusion on gives both clients `232e8f4e` cold and `74d484f8` prime/warm, NOT gold, at 6.74/6.74/7.11 aggregate tok/s. N=2 fusion off gives both clients `400f1b3bb1` cold and `7669259d` prime/warm, at 6.40/6.80/7.11 aggregate tok/s. The fused matvec sites split at N=2: `FUSED_Q split=508 batched=0`, `FUSED_ID_Q split=224 batched=0`. Batched `MUL_MAT`/`MUL_MAT_ID` calls at N=2 (1798/444) are exactly twice N=1's (899/222): the two prompts' prefill ubatches, as standalone. These are host-call counters, including capture and excluding replay. Exact dispatch therefore engages in real fused runs, refuting fd10's splitter-bypass reading. With fusion off, N=2 reproduces fusion-off N=1 bytes (`400f1b3bb1` is the N=1 fusion-off md5 measured in fdg), so exact mode is self-consistent across N when nothing is fused. With fusion on, N=2 equals plain batching byte for byte, pointing to a fused kernel outside the matvec sites (norm, add chain, rope+set_rows, router top-k, or unary) that computes differently with 2 rows than with 1. A per-fusion-class bisection, window fdb, is in design. Exact N=2 gives about 6.7–7.1 aggregate tok/s (+22–28 % over single-stream) with fusion on or off. **The per-column exact dispatch engages in real runs (counters); exact N=2 is bit-identical across N when fusion is off and gains +22-28 % aggregate. With fusion on one non-matvec fused kernel differs at 2 rows; bisection by fusion class next (fdb).**

**Batching, the culprit: the MoE weighted reduction.** Window fdb (01:12) tests the fd9 tree with 0035 v7: 17 per-class fusion-disable switches through `GGML_CUDA_DISABLE_FUSION_CLASSES`, default off (fusion remains enabled). The standard request is the 10-token prompt plus 128 generated tokens, `temperature=0`, `seed=1`, ubatch 2048. All exact flags (`GGML_CUDA_EXACT_COLS=1 GGML_CUDA_MMVQ_EXACT_COLS=1 LLAMA_EXACT_ATTN_COLS=1 GGML_CUDA_ARGSORT_EXACT_ROWS=1`) plus `LLAMA_PREFILL_ISOLATE=1 LLAMA_EXPERT_STORE_SPLIT_COLS=1` are set. Each present class is disabled alone at N=1 and N=2; the two N=2 clients always have identical bytes. [legs-fdb.txt](evidence/glm-windows-2026-10-30/fdb/legs-fdb.txt): md5 prefixes with none disabled are N=1 `9449ae5738` (gold), N=2 `232e8f4e`; all fusion off gives N=1 = N=2 = `400f1b3bb1`. Disabling `rms_norm`, `rms_norm_rope`, `rope_set_rows`, `multi_add`, `multi_mul`, `unary_mul`, `matvec` or `shared_expert` alone has no effect: N=1 gold, N=2 `232e8f4e`. Disabling `moe_weighted_reduction` alone gives N=1 = N=2 = `f451f8a585` (EQUAL, not production gold). Disabling `topk_moe` alone gives N=1 `232e8f4e` (the N=2 fused bytes), N=2 `b5933beb` (plain batching's other column). Seven absent GLM graph classes were skipped: `rms_norm_scale`, `ssm_conv`, `gdn_cache`, `ssm_scan_cache`, `snake`, `relu_sqr`, `softcap`. The fused MoE weighted reduction is the router-weighted sum of the 8 expert outputs, fused with the top-k routing chain. It is the single class whose removal makes N=2 equal N=1. N=1 with top-k fusion off equals N=2 fused, indicating that at two tokens the fused router/merge chain runs a different arithmetic path. The fix is in design: 0035 v8 launches the N=1 fused chain per token under exact mode; window fdr will test it. **Culprit found: the fused MoE weighted reduction (with the top-k routing fusion). Every other fusion class is row-exact. Fix: per-token fused launch in exact mode (0035 v8, window fdr).**

**Batching, exact and faster.** Window fdr (01:24) tests the updated Spark (driver 580.178.04, kernel 7.0), using the fd9 tree: 0030 v3 N-column split, 0008 exact MMVQ columns, 0034 v2 digest observer present but off, 0035 v8 (per-column fused-site launches, hot-path flag caching, and the MoE weighted-reduction fix), 0036 v5 per-stream attention extents, 0037 prefill isolation, 0039 guards, and 0040 graph budget. All exact flags (`GGML_CUDA_EXACT_COLS=1 GGML_CUDA_MMVQ_EXACT_COLS=1 LLAMA_EXACT_ATTN_COLS=1 GGML_CUDA_ARGSORT_EXACT_ROWS=1`) plus `LLAMA_PREFILL_ISOLATE=1 LLAMA_EXPERT_STORE_SPLIT_COLS=1` are set, with fusion enabled and `--batch-size 2048 --ubatch-size 2048`. Requests use the standard 10-token prompt + 128 generated tokens and a 2048-token long prompt. The split-bank N>1 expert view has nine rows per token for eight experts; this made the fused weighted-reduction matcher reject it at split layers and fall back to the unfused merge. V8 accepts the token-strided view under exact mode and calls the N=1 fused fold per token. Evidence: [legs-fdr.txt](evidence/glm-windows-2026-10-30/fdr/legs-fdr.txt) and [runner58-fdr.txt](evidence/glm-windows-2026-10-30/fdr/runner58-fdr.txt). N=1 is production gold `9449ae5738` at 5.03 aggregate tok/s. N=2 has both clients GOLD in all three rounds, at 7.07 / 7.16 / 7.14 aggregate tok/s. The long N=1 reference is `e38742e4` at 4.07 aggregate tok/s including prefill; N=2 short+long matches both respective references at 4.75 aggregate tok/s, dominated by long prefill. With `--parallel 4` and only two active clients, both are gold at 7.08 aggregate tok/s; this is not a four-client throughput measurement. Every tested client in the batch receives byte-identical output to a single-sequence production run, while two concurrent streams deliver about +40 % aggregate throughput over one. Four- and eight-client throughput is next in window fdq on a clean tree with the hot-path patches and without the debug observer. **Exact batching achieved: N=2 is bit-identical to the production gold for every client in every round, at +40 % aggregate throughput (7.1 vs 5.0 tok/s). N=4/8 throughput next (fdq), then a production decision.**

**Batching, exact throughput.** Window fdq (01:43) measures exact-mode throughput at N=1/2/4/8 on the updated Spark (driver 580.178.04, kernel 7.0). `~/llama-k3-fdq` is the clean production x3 tree + 0030 v3hot + 0008.hot + 0035 v8 + 0036 v5hot + 0037.hot + 0038 + 0039.hot + 0040.hot, freshly built with no digest observer. It uses the production composition env, cache 84000 MiB, total context 65536, and `--batch-size 2048 --ubatch-size 2048`. Exact legs set `GGML_CUDA_EXACT_COLS=1 GGML_CUDA_MMVQ_EXACT_COLS=1 LLAMA_EXACT_ATTN_COLS=1 GGML_CUDA_ARGSORT_EXACT_ROWS=1 LLAMA_EXPERT_STORE_SPLIT_COLS=1 LLAMA_PREFILL_ISOLATE=1`. Each leg starts a fresh server, runs a warm round and three measured rounds, and gates every client byte for byte against its single-sequence reference; requests use the standard 10-token prompt + 128 generated tokens and a 2048-token long prompt. Evidence: [legs-fdq.txt](evidence/glm-windows-2026-10-30/fdq/legs-fdq.txt) and [runner59-fdq.txt](evidence/glm-windows-2026-10-30/fdq/runner59-fdq.txt). Aggregate tok/s in the three measured rounds: Q1 N=1 without flags 5.47 / 5.55 / 5.58; Q1x N=1 with all flags 5.42 / 5.55 / 5.58 (no cost); Q2 N=2 7.14 / 7.14 / 7.15 (+29 %); Q4 N=4 8.16 / 8.09 / 8.06 (+46 %); Q8 N=8 8.72 / 8.72 / 8.71 (+57 %). Q4m (two short + two long) gives 6.21 / 6.18 / 6.21; Q2s (second client 5 s later) 6.20 / 6.22 / 6.22; Q2w (both warm) 6.653 / 7.145 / 7.168. The long N=1 reference is 4.11–4.46 including prefill. There are 102 client completions marked PASS: every client in Q1, Q1x, Q2, Q4, Q8, Q4m and Q2s matches. The six Q2w lines marked FAIL are a gate error: warm requests (`cache_prompt=true` after priming) were compared with the cold gold, while both clients returned `f675805e`, the standalone warm reference from every N=1 warm run (fdw, fd8, fd9, fd11). Warm N=2 is exact too; the raw FAIL lines and resulting nonzero status are retained in the evidence. The stats leg confirms all fused matvec sites split: `FUSED_Q split=528 batched=0`, `FUSED_ID_Q split=224 batched=0`. Exact batching holds for every tested client at N=2/4/8, with cold and warm requests, equal and unequal prompts, and staggered arrivals covered by the legs above. Aggregate throughput reaches +57 % at N=8 but flattens beyond N=4 because each expert is launched once per column, so host-bank expert traffic grows with N (m2); plain non-exact batching reached 17–19.6 aggregate tok/s at N=8. Per-stream rates fall to ~3.6 (N=2), ~2.0 (N=4), ~1.1 (N=8). Prefill isolation serialises prompts, and context per slot is 65536/N. **Exact batching on the production stack: every client bit-identical at N=2/4/8, +29 % / +46 % / +57 % aggregate over single-stream, flags free at N=1. Production candidate: --parallel 4 (or 2 for per-user latency), pending the owner's go.**

**Batching, context per slot.** Window fdc (11:16) tests context per slot at `--parallel 2`, using the fdq build (clean production x3 + 0030 v3hot + 0008.hot + 0035 v8 + 0036 v5hot + 0037.hot + 0038 + 0039.hot + 0040.hot, no digest observer), the production composition env and all six knobs: `GGML_CUDA_EXACT_COLS=1 GGML_CUDA_MMVQ_EXACT_COLS=1 LLAMA_EXACT_ATTN_COLS=1 GGML_CUDA_ARGSORT_EXACT_ROWS=1 LLAMA_EXPERT_STORE_SPLIT_COLS=1 LLAMA_PREFILL_ISOLATE=1`. C64 uses `--ctx-size 131072 --moe-cache-mib 78000` (65536 tokens per slot); C128 uses `--ctx-size 262144 --moe-cache-mib 66105` (131072 per slot), with cache scaled from C64's memory low point. Each configuration runs N=1, N=2 (two standard clients), and a long prompt (48000 tokens for C64, 100000 for C128) beside a decoding standard client, followed by the same long request alone as its reference. Evidence: [legs-fdc.txt](evidence/glm-windows-2026-10-30/fdc/legs-fdc.txt) and [runner60-fdc.txt](evidence/glm-windows-2026-10-30/fdc/runner60-fdc.txt). KV allocation is 11904 MiB for C64 and 23808 MiB for C128: 5952 MiB per 65536 cells, about 93 KiB per token. C64 N=1 is gold `9449ae5738` at 5.24 / 5.30 / 5.28 aggregate tok/s, -4.5 % versus production's 5.47–5.58 at cache 84000 MiB. C64 N=2 has both clients gold every round at 7.02 / 7.03 / 7.02, -1.7 % versus fdq's 7.14 at cache 84000 MiB; memory low is 5.8–6.0 GB, above the 4.29 GB reserve. The C64 48k prompt prefilled at ~113 tok/s beside the decoding client (the completed request reports 107.7 tok/s overall); that client's bytes stayed gold while decode stalled to 0.26 tok/s during prefill, consistent with prefill isolation. The standalone reference was killed at 81 % by the window's own 900 s server cap: two 48k prefills do not fit in 900 s, with no server error or memory pressure causing the stop (the client reports a missing final SSE event). A rerun with a 2400 s cap is pending, so long-request byte equality against its standalone reference is unverified. C128 N=1 returns md5 `9a76bcc9`, NOT the production gold, at 4.40–4.49 aggregate tok/s (-19 %); N=2 returns `9a76bcc9` for both clients every round at 6.30 / 6.52 / 6.50 (-9–12 % versus fdq N=2). Memory falls to 4.07 GB, below reserve, with one valve release of 1.31 GB; the C128 long leg hits the same server cap. Thus 2 x 64k preserves today's 65536-token context per request and gold bytes, at -4.5 % single-stream and +27 % aggregate with two users relative to production single-stream. At 2 x 128k, bytes depend on the configured context size even for a single sequence: the output is self-consistent across N but is a different gold; the op that reads the context size is not yet identified. It costs 9–19 % and runs memory below the valve reserve. **--parallel 2 with 65536 tokens per slot (ctx 131072, cache 78000 MiB) is gold and costs 4.5 % single-stream for +27 % with two users; 128k per slot changes the bytes (context-size-dependent gold) and costs 9-19 %.**

**Batching, long prompt per slot (rerun).** Window fdcl (12:03) reruns the C64 long leg of fdc with a 2400 s server cap (same build, env, six knobs, `--ctx-size 131072 --parallel 2 --moe-cache-mib 78000`). Evidence: [legs-fdcl.txt](evidence/glm-windows-2026-10-30/fdc/legs-fdcl.txt) and [runner62-fdcl.txt](evidence/glm-windows-2026-10-30/fdc/runner62-fdcl.txt). In the warm round and round 1 the 48000-token prompt, prefilled beside a decoding standard client, returns md5 `1a3356a4` and its standalone reference returns the same `1a3356a4` (LONG-GATE PASS both rounds); the short client is gold `9449ae5738` in all three rounds while it stalls to 0.26-0.28 tok/s during the ~420-447 s prefill (prefill isolation). Long prefill runs at 107-115 tok/s. Round 2's standalone reference was killed by the 2400 s cap again (two 48k prefills plus decode exceed it), which is the leg's nonzero status; memory stayed at 5.73 GB lowest against the 4.29 GB reserve. **2 x 64k per slot holds gold for a 48k prompt mixed with a decoding client: the long request is byte-identical to its standalone run.**

**gpt-oss-120b on the Spark (ops fallback candidate).** Window gpt (13:01) stops production GLM, runs llama-server with `gpt-oss-120b-MXFP4.gguf` (the original MXFP4 weights), measures a 2048- and a 16384-token prompt with 128 generated tokens, checks tool calls on `/v1/chat/completions` and native `/v1/messages`, and restores the GLM pod. Evidence: [runner63-gpt.txt](evidence/glm-windows-2026-10-30/gpt/runner63-gpt.txt). Prefill 2266.5 tok/s at 2048 and 2765.6 tok/s at 16384; decode 54.7 tok/s (18.29 ms/token) after the 2048 prompt and 48.5 tok/s (20.62 ms/token) after 16384. `CHAT tool_calls True` and `MESSAGES tool_use True` with round trips of 0.40 and 0.85 s. **gpt-oss-120b runs at ~10x GLM's decode and ~20x its prefill on the Spark with working tool calls on both APIs; quality comparison pending the ops eval.**

**GH200: run-to-run nondeterminism found and fixed (2026-11-01/02).** On gh200-dev (sm_90, 64 KiB pages, GLM-5.3 Q2 fully resident: v1 store + full snapshot, 260000 MiB host slots, GPU slot maps, split GEMV, prefill bank off, HBM tier off) the standard request gave a different md5 on almost every request, in every configuration of the earlier bisection, including inside one process. Tooling: a native sm_90 debug tree (`~/llama-det`: production patches + 0039-0041 + the 0034 digest observer, extended with `LLAMA_DEBUG_TENSOR_PREFILL=1` to digest prompt graphs and `LLAMA_DEBUG_TENSOR_SLOTS=1` to hash the expert-slot bytes each MUL_MAT_ID reads, once before and once after it runs) and [reqdiff.py](evidence/glm-windows-2026-10-30/gh200/reqdiff.py), which aligns requests inside one digest file. Evidence: [det-summary.txt](evidence/glm-windows-2026-10-30/gh200/det-summary.txt) (every run's md5, tok/s and relevant env), [dig2-reqdiff.txt](evidence/glm-windows-2026-10-30/gh200/dig2-reqdiff.txt), [xp-reqdiff.txt](evidence/glm-windows-2026-10-30/gh200/xp-reqdiff.txt), scripts [det-native.sh](evidence/glm-windows-2026-10-30/gh200/det-native.sh) and [prefill-bench.sh](evidence/glm-windows-2026-10-30/gh200/prefill-bench.sh). (1) In one process, two requests first differed in the prompt prefill at a layer's `ffn_moe_up` (MUL_MAT_ID) output while its activations, slot ids and slot bytes were identical (pre/post slot hashes: 0 mismatches). Cause: patch 0003's `k_mmq_compact_experts` builds the expert list with `atomicAdd`, so its order varies, and MMQ's stream-k split walks the list in order, moving each expert's K split points. Patch [0042](../patches/0042-ordered-expert-list.patch) compacts in a fixed order: 5/5 requests in one process byte-identical (`b533bf356d`, 22.9 tok/s). (2) Across processes the bytes still differed (`b533bf356d`, `b5933beb88`; with `GGML_CUDA_DETERMINISTIC=1` (0041: fusion and TMA off) still `782b427aa2`, `5ad1d41f2d`, `411325f13d`; with one restore thread still `782b427aa2`, `541ad7e08c`). The cross-process digest first differs at `ffn_moe_slots-3`: same experts, different slots, so list positions and stream-k splits still differ. Patch [0043](../patches/0043-mmq-id-tiling.patch) adds `GGML_CUDA_MMQ_ID_TILING=1`: the MoE MMQ kernel runs one block per tile (stream-k's tiling mode), so each tile is summed over its full K in one order. Five processes (tile-a/b/c, pf-tile, pf-bank) all give `c592f33e7c`, two requests each where measured. The address-dependent dispatch hypothesis (fusion overlap check, TMA alignment) is not needed to explain the GH200 results. (3) Cost: none measured. A 24438-token prompt prefills at 584.7/585.1 tok/s without and 584.7/584.8 with the switch; decode 23.0 tok/s both. The prefill bank now works on 64 KiB pages with 0039 but is slower here (567.5/568.2 tok/s), as expected with every expert already resident, so it stays off. `c592f33e7c` is the GH200 reference for the standard request; it is not the Spark gold (different prefill path and architecture). **GH200 nondeterminism was an unordered expert list plus slot-position-dependent stream-k splits, not hardware: 0042 + `GGML_CUDA_MMQ_ID_TILING=1` (0043) make the GH200 byte-identical across requests and processes at no measured cost.**

**GH200 decode profile.** Nsight Systems (2024.6, Lambda Stack package; it needs `LD_LIBRARY_PATH` for its injection library, `--kill none`, and the importer run by hand) on the debug tree with the production env and `GGML_CUDA_MMQ_ID_TILING=1`, 5 s of steady decode (22.67 tok/s under the profiler, about 113 tokens). Evidence: [kernsum.txt](evidence/glm-windows-2026-10-30/gh200/kernsum.txt), [kern.csv](evidence/glm-windows-2026-10-30/gh200/kern.csv), [prof-decode.sh](evidence/glm-windows-2026-10-30/gh200/prof-decode.sh). GPU kernel time is 5072.6 ms of the 5 s window: the GPU is saturated and there are no CPU-side gaps, so CUDA graphs and hook latency are not the bottleneck (decode was 21.3 tok/s with graphs off and with them on). The routed-expert GEMVs (`mul_mat_vec_q` types 17 and 18, IQ2_XS and IQ3_XXS, weights read from host memory over NVLink-C2C) take 51 % (83 and 81 us per launch, ~23 ms per token), i.e. 350-475 GB/s against the measured 485-528 GB/s for registered host reads and ~450 GB/s per direction physical. The rest (~22 ms per token) is the HBM-resident spine in many small kernels (Q5_K/Q8_0 GEMVs, 1058 `quantize_q8_1` launches per token at 2.2 us, flash attention, norms). **GH200 decode is GPU-bound at ~45 ms/token: half is expert reads at ~75 % of C2C bandwidth, half is small-kernel spine work. Levers: hot experts in HBM, fewer and larger spine kernels.**

**GH200 HBM expert tier.** With 0042 + 0043 in the shipped stack (sm_90 build of production 0001-0038 + 0042 + 0043, `~/llama-ship`), full residency, ctx 131072 and `GGML_CUDA_MMQ_ID_TILING=1`, the adaptive HBM tier (`LLAMA_EXPERT_STORE_HBM_MIB`, LFU, decay 512) was measured over two processes x four standard requests per size. Evidence: [hbm-summary.txt](evidence/glm-windows-2026-10-30/gh200/hbm-summary.txt). Tier off: 22.79 / 22.85 tok/s. 24576 MiB: 29.62-30.29 tok/s. 32768 MiB: 30.41-31.02 tok/s. 49152 MiB (with the 24438-token prefill benchmark): prefill 606.8 / 607.2 tok/s (585 without the tier), decode 32.13 tok/s, peak GPU memory 80343 MiB of 97871. Every request in every size returns `c592f33e7c`, the same bytes as tier off: the tier's earlier run-to-run differences were the unordered expert list. The tier turns the split GEMV path off by design. **48 GiB HBM tier: decode 22.8 -> 32.1 tok/s (+41 %), prefill +4 %, bytes unchanged; adopted for the GH200 deployment.**

**GH200 throughput workup (2026-11-02).** Native run of the shipped stack (`~/llama-ship`: 0001-0038 + 0042 + 0043, sm_90) with the production manifest's env (argo-things `71e4639`: 48 GiB HBM tier, `GGML_CUDA_MMQ_ID_TILING=1`, lean hooks, graphs on) and args, except `--cache-ram 0` and `--parallel N`; ctx 131072; one fresh server per leg; requests `ignore_eos` so every stream generates exactly 256 tokens; power from `nvidia-smi` every 0.5 s (GPU and module = Grace CPU + Hopper GPU + memory). Evidence: [bench/](evidence/glm-windows-2026-10-30/gh200/bench/) (bench.py, bench-run.sh, analyze.py, results.jsonl, summary.json, power.csv.gz). Single stream by context (prefill / decode tok/s): 1026 tokens 553.2 / 30.28; 4113: 682.8 / 31.81; 16414: 588.1 / 32.94; 32617: 615.8 / 32.65; 65243: 594.9 / 31.67; 119734: 542.3 / 28.81 (the 120k prompt reads in 220.8 s). Concurrency (aggregate tok/s, per stream, module W, tokens per kJ): plain N=1 29.93 / 30.68 / 480 W / 62.3; N=2 58.22 / 29.77 / 521 W / 111.7; N=4 75.75 / 19.25 / 540 W / 140.3; N=8 88.87 / 11.27 / 552 W / 161.1; N=16 28.74 / 1.79 / 397 W / 72.4 (cliff: above 8 columns MUL_MAT_ID leaves MMVQ for MMQ, which is slow with host-resident weights and one or two tokens per expert). Exact mode (six knobs) N=1/2/4/8: 31.07 / 26.75 / 28.61 / 29.61 aggregate, every stream byte-identical to the single-stream output `6d9d4fa70a` (256 forced tokens); plain batching changes the bytes. Exact batching works on the GH200 but gives no throughput: with every expert resident, its per-column expert launches re-read the same weights, which is the whole cost (on the Spark the gain came from amortised NVMe reads). Module idles at ~208 W. **GH200 GLM-5.3 Q2: 30.7 tok/s single stream, 28.8 at 120k context, ~600 tok/s prefill at 1k-120k, 88.9 tok/s aggregate at 8 plain streams (161 tok/kJ module); exact multi-stream holds bytes but not speed; 16 streams collapse to 29.**

**Spark at 128k context (window lc, 2026-11-02).** The owner wants long context on the Spark (Claude Code and OpenClaw). Native run with the fdq build (exact knobs unset, so the production path) and the glm53-spark manifest's env, one slot. Evidence: [legs-lc.txt](evidence/glm-windows-2026-10-30/lc/legs-lc.txt), [results.jsonl](evidence/glm-windows-2026-10-30/lc/results.jsonl), [runner64-lc.txt](evidence/glm-windows-2026-10-30/lc/runner64-lc.txt), [win2lc.sh](evidence/glm-windows-2026-10-30/lc/win2lc.sh), [lc_client.py](evidence/glm-windows-2026-10-30/lc/lc_client.py). LC-64 (ctx 65536, cache 84000, production): standard request gold `9449ae5738` at 5.118 / 5.700 tok/s; KV 5616 + 336 MiB. LC-128 (ctx 131072, cache 78000): standard request gold `9449ae5738` at 4.904 / 5.472 / 5.556 tok/s; KV 11232 + 672 MiB. Depth (cache off, 64 forced tokens): 8189 tokens prefill 105.78 tok/s / decode 4.111; 32617: 102.43 / 5.663; 65243: 100.17 / 5.99; 99676: 97.37 / 5.815 (the 100k prompt takes 1024 s). Follow-up turn: with the 65243-token prompt in the slot, the same prompt plus ~320 new tokens processed 320 tokens (cache_n 65243) in 10.4 s. So: one 128k slot is still gold (the 2 x 128k byte change in fdc came from the 262144-cell configuration, not from 128k per slot), costs ~3 % at short context, decode stays flat with depth, prefill falls only ~8 % from 8k to 100k, and a returning turn pays only its new tokens while it keeps the slot. **128k on the Spark: gold, ~3 % slower, flat decode to 100k; adopted (argo-things PR #92).**

**GH200 at 512k context (2026-11-02).** OpenClaw hit compaction after three messages at 128k (its first turn alone is 84,902 tokens). Sweep on the shipped stack with the production env, glm53-gh200 scaled to 0 (evidence: [ctx512/](evidence/glm-windows-2026-10-30/gh200/ctx512/), sweep-ctx.log): ctx 262144 / HBM tier 40960 MiB: peak HBM 86099 MiB, 99,676-token prompt read at 553.0 tok/s, decode at that depth 24.07, standard request 27.57 tok/s; 393216 / 28672: 87765 MiB, 551.8, 26.23, 28.09; 524288 / 16384: 86677 MiB, 538.2, 25.84, 27.63; all `c592f33e7c`. Full-depth check at 524288 / 16384 with `--cache-ram 98304` (deep512.log): a 457,428-token prompt read in 1408.3 s (324.8 tok/s; the DSA indexer's per-token cost grows with depth), decode at that depth 17.04 tok/s; a 101,669-token prompt next (543.7 tok/s) pushed it into the host prompt cache; the first prompt plus 324 new tokens then came back with `cache_n` 457,428, reading only the 324 tokens (2.3 s, 5.3 s wall), decode 17.48. Peak HBM 89,314 MiB of 97,871, lowest host MemAvailable 164 GiB. Deployed (argo-things #94, with #90 removing the thinking cap); live standard request `c592f33e7c` at 25.8 / 27.8 tok/s. Short requests are ~15 % slower than the 128k / 48 GiB setup (27.6 vs 32.1). **GH200 serves 512k: decode 26-28 short, 17 at 457k, a returning turn pays only its new tokens from the 96 GiB prompt cache. Next: elastic KV (map KV pages as the context grows, shrink the expert tier only then) to give short conversations the 48 GiB tier back.**

**GH200 MTP speculative decoding (2026-11-03).** llama.cpp's `--spec-type draft-mtp` with GLM-5.3's own next-token layer (blk.78), 512k production settings, one server per config, three requests each (quicksort at temperature 0 and 1, and a chat step that returns a tool call). Evidence: [mtp/](evidence/glm-windows-2026-10-30/gh200/mtp/). No drafting: 25.07 / 26.21 / 25.29 tok/s. n_max 1: 8.84 (first request, warm-up) / 21.59 / 26.27, acceptance 0.89 / 0.61 / 1.00. n_max 2: 27.62 / 24.28 / 33.92, acceptance 0.76 / 0.49 / 1.00, peak HBM 93,002 MiB. n_max 3: 26.45 / 23.91 / 32.51, acceptance 0.68 / 0.53 / 0.97. Unconfident drafts make temperature-1 prose slower. With `--spec-draft-p-min 0.7`: n_max 2: 27.91 / 28.89 / 30.52 (acceptance 0.94 / 0.93 / 1.00); n_max 3: 29.10 / 29.06 / 30.07 (0.90 / 0.84 / 0.87), +11-19 %. Depth check, n_max 3 + p-min 0.7 + HBM tier 12288 MiB: a 457,428-token prompt reads at 301.4 tok/s (325 without MTP at 16 GiB), decode at that depth 15.54 (17.04); a 101,669-token prompt 516.2 (543.7), decode 26.78 (24.94); prompt-cache return 324 new tokens in 2.7 s; peak HBM 88,891 MiB. The long prompts are random words, so drafts were rare there and the depth numbers mostly show MTP's overhead plus the smaller tier. Side effect: the native servers shared the pod's snapshot file; the MTP run saved one the pod rejected ("made for another store or model"), the pod started cold (5.69 tok/s) and overwrote the file. Rebuilt from the seed with full-snapshot-mtp.py; native tests now use a copy. **MTP with a 0.7 confidence floor: +11-19 % decode at short context, +8 % at 100k, -9 % at 457k, prefill -5-7 %. Not deployed (mixed for Juno's ~200k contexts); branch glm53-gh200-mtp kept.**

**GH200 prefill profile (2026-11-03).** Nsight Systems on the shipped stack with the production 512k env (16 GiB HBM tier, `GGML_CUDA_MMQ_ID_TILING=1`), one 203,198-token prompt (cache off, read at 453.5 tok/s in 448 s), two 15 s captures: shallow (~10-20k tokens in) and deep (~170k in). Evidence: [prefill-prof/](evidence/glm-windows-2026-10-30/gh200/prefill-prof/). GPU kernel time is 14.45 / 14.55 s of each 15 s window: prefill is GPU-bound. Shallow: routed-expert MMQ 50.8 % (IQ2_XS gate/up 7.13 ms per launch, IQ3_XXS down 8.52 ms; ~22.8 ms per MoE layer per 2048-token ubatch for ~3.1 GB of expert weights read from host memory = ~137 GB/s effective, a third of the C2C link), flash attention 16.0 % + 2.8 %, DSA lightning indexer 7.3 % (11.3 ms per call), Q5_K/Q8_0 spine MMQ ~11 %. Deep: lightning indexer 37.8 % (93.3 ms per call, ~8x shallow: the indexer scores every query against every earlier key, so prefill cost per token grows with depth), routed experts 31.6 %, flash attention 11.0 %. Prefill speed fell from 568 tok/s at 20k to 468 at 184k. **GH200 prefill is GPU-bound: shallow it is the expert MMQ streaming host-resident weights at ~137 GB/s (levers: stage each layer's experts into HBM ahead of its MMQ); deep it is the DSA lightning indexer (a kernel lever).**

**GLM-5.3-Flash on the Spark (windows flash, flash2, flash3, 2026-11-03).** The owner asked for GLM-5.3-Flash on the Spark with the full context for Juno. Model: unsloth UD-Q2_K_XL, 108.7 GB, SHA-256 verified; glm5-next, 46 blocks (45 + 1 MTP), hidden 4096, 288 experts (8 + 1 shared), expert FFN 2048, 3 leading dense layers, KDA linear attention in 3 of every 4 layers with MLA + DSA indexer (top_k 2048) in the 4th, 1,048,576-token context, hyper-connections. Evidence: [flash/](evidence/glm-windows-2026-10-30/flash/). Window flash (fully resident, `-ngl 999`, no store): at ctx 1048576 the load ran the box out of memory (kernel OOM killer took a containerd shim; kimi-k3-spark-dma-latency restarted 5 times; the runner's ssh dropped and it restored glm53-spark into the stall); with `--load-mode mmap` and a 4 GiB MemAvailable watchdog, 524288 was killed by the watchdog at 1.4 GB during tensor load. Expert store: repack-experts.py, 43 MoE layers x 288 experts, 101.66 GB, 64 random records verified. Window flash2 (store, cache 70000 MiB, ctx 1048576): KV 11,264 MiB MLA + 8,448 MiB indexer + 146 MiB recurrent state, 6,154 MiB dense weights on the GPU; one slot hit the watchdog at 3.7 GB MemAvailable; four slots (1M split four ways) loaded: 31.7 (warm) / 42.6 / 35.9 tok/s aggregate, 8.3-10.9 per stream, hit rate 85.7 %. Window flash3 (store, cache 58000 MiB, one 1M slot): standard 256-token request 10.86 (warm) / 12.67 / 12.57 / 12.93 tok/s; prompts of 2,068 / 16,414 / 65,243 tokens read at 273.9 / 303.9 / 293.1 tok/s with decode after them 9.90 / 11.37 / 11.33 tok/s; tool call: `kubectl_get {"kind":"pods","namespace":"garage"}` after 142 characters of reasoning; four slots 28.8 (warm) / 37.0 / 39.8 tok/s aggregate; hit rate 89.9 %; lowest MemAvailable 10,972 MiB. **GLM-5.3-Flash on the Spark with the full 1M context: 12.7 tok/s single stream (2.3x GLM-5.3 there), ~300 tok/s prompts (3x), 40 tok/s with four streams; needs the expert store (58 GB cache) to fit. Proposed for production (argo-things PR #123).**

**nuc2 expert tier for GLM-5.3-Flash: latency prototype (2026-11-03).** The owner asked to place the experts the Spark cannot hold on nuc2 with minimal network traffic (activations only). First the ceiling: production glm53-spark (Flash, 58 GB cache) reports hit rate 88.6 %, 7.1 of 8 experts complete at the early lookup, 0.87 records read under the early matmul and a 0.43 ms wait at the real lookup per MoE layer: 43 x 0.43 = ~18.5 ms of a ~79 ms token, so no remote tier can add more than ~30 %. Prototype (evidence: [flash-nuc2/](evidence/glm-windows-2026-10-30/flash-nuc2/)): `moebench` with configurable D/F (4096/2048) and a busy-poll receive (`MOEBENCH_SPIN=1`), CPU backend (alderlake variant), layer 20's 288 Flash records (IQ2_XS gate/up, IQ3_XXS down, 8,060,928 B each, extracted from the store) on nuc2; `rrclient` with `RR_D=4096` on the Spark. nuc2 compute alone, 4 threads: k=1 0.777 ms median (p90 1.242), k=2 0.867, k=4 1.761 ms; 8 threads no better. Round trip Spark -> nuc2 (ping 0.19 ms): k=1 p50 0.953 / p90 1.464 / p99 2.445 ms, k=2 1.403 / 1.956, k=3 1.663 / 2.248. The network and software add only ~0.2 ms; the rest is nuc2's CPU computing one 8 MB expert at ~10 GB/s, no faster than the Spark reading that record from its own NVMe (~0.7-1.1 ms), most of which already overlaps the early matmul. A first-answer-wins hedge might trim the tail for an estimated +5-8 %, and would need nuc2 kernels bit-identical to the GPU's for IQ2_XS/IQ3_XXS/IQ4_XS with clamped SwiGLU (the owner chose bit-exactness). **nuc2 expert tier for Flash: not built. The round trip is bound by nuc2's single-expert compute, not by the 2.5 GbE link, so faster networking does not change the verdict; elastic KV (lend the unused part of the 20 GB 1M KV to the expert cache) is the better lever.**

**Prefill bank in production (2026-10-31 afternoon).** Image `g72518b8` ships [patch 0020](../patches/0020-prefill-bank.patch) (the ledger's 0010) with `LLAMA_EXPERT_STORE_PREFILL_BANK=1`, cache 84000 MiB and LFU (argo-things `5df13e2`). Live checks through the public endpoint immediately after rollout ([prodcheck4.txt](evidence/glm-windows-2026-10-30/prodcheck4.txt), [prefillcheck1.txt](evidence/glm-windows-2026-10-30/prefillcheck1.txt)) gave decode **5.254 cold / 5.755 warm tok/s**, md5 `9449ae5738`. A 2048-token prompt (the windows' generator text tokenised by the server, `cache_prompt: false`) prefilled at **108.7 / 109.2 tok/s** in two passes, versus ~72 on the previous production image; the 32 tokens decoded after it ran at **2.65 / 3.52 tok/s** (cold context), md5 `aa6d55b8e3` both passes. There is no prior reference for this exact fixture; window x3 gates on the same binary matched the long-prompt golds. The pod's valve line reported host MemAvailable lowest **6.90 GB** against the **4.29 GB** reserve, **0 releases**. Stack incident: GH200-only patch 0004b (CPU-slot-map guard) entered `patches/` with the GH200 prep (`aa52f22`) and had been part of production image `ge4e4290` and subsequent images, and the window trees' stack checks since then. It shifted the prefill-bank patch's context and was moved to `tools/gh200/` in `72518b8`. Spark uses GPU slot maps, so 0004b changes nothing there; the measurements stand.

**Huge-page bank in production.** Argo-things `8e9d8b6` set `LLAMA_EXPERT_STORE_THP_BANK=1` on image `g72518b8`, with cache 84000 MiB, LFU and the prefill bank. The startup [pod log](evidence/glm-windows-2026-10-30/thp-prod.txt) reports three banks with huge backing **82480988160/82480988160 bytes (100.00 %)**, **1560281088/1560281088 bytes (100.00 %)** and **4018143232/4022337536 bytes (99.90 %)**. On the third bank, `COLLAPSE` returned `-1 errno 11` (`EAGAIN`); the gate warned and continued, as review round 14 required. The memory valve reached a lowest **6.67 GB** against the **4.29 GB** reserve, with **0 releases**. The [live check immediately after rollout](evidence/glm-windows-2026-10-30/prodcheck5.txt) measured **5.213 cold / 5.736 warm tok/s**, md5 `9449ae5738`, versus the previous image’s check of **5.254 / 5.755 tok/s**. The window’s **+1.3 %** is below this live check’s resolution; the production number stays **~5.75 warm tok/s**.

**Predictor study on the real trace.** The [study](evidence/glm-windows-2026-10-30/RESULT-PREDICTOR.md) uses 2048 tokens, layers 3–77, with kind 1 storing 256 predictor f32 scores (layers 4–77), kind 2 storing 256 biased router f32 scores, kind 3 storing eight real i32 IDs, kind 4 storing 256 unbiased router f32 scores, and kind 5 storing one generated token i32; no kinds 6–9 are present. Tokens 1024–2047 are evaluated after warming a simulated 80000 MiB LRU with 12.09 MB records (12,091,392 bytes; 6937 slots), with a shared budget of five missing reads per source layer. Baseline top8 accounting per layer is `a/b/c` = 6.0 / 1.46 / 0.55: previously resident / real consumed early read / real demand miss. The Spark's measured exposed count is ~1.35 per layer; the simulator's 0.55 is not fitted. Completion is instantaneous; prefill cache, pinning, async cancellation and partial-read timing are not modeled. Useful means consumed speculative reads / speculative reads; reads include demand and speculative reads.

| candidate | reads/token | useful % | c/layer |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| top8 (baseline) | 196.38 | 70.5 | 0.554 |
| top10-cut | 233.81 | 59.2 | 0.418 |
| sticky-union | 196.38 | 70.5 | 0.554 |
| sticky-rerank | 179.81 | 75.2 | 0.794 |
| prior-rerank | 193.26 | 71.4 | 0.570 |
| prior-only | 150.35 | 2.9 | 1.954 |
| two-ahead-spare | 196.38 | 70.5 | 0.554 |
| top8-cut | 168.97 | 80.9 | 0.854 |

None beats the baseline's exposure without reading more: top10-cut saves about 10 exposed records per token for 37 extra reads; sticky-union and two-ahead-spare tie the baseline, while the other candidates save reads but expose more records. Trace top-8 precision is 79.1 % versus the engine's 85.2 % in window p; the trace's predictor tensor cannot be tied to an input mode. Previous-token same-layer reselection is 21.7 %. **Predictor input 3 stays; the exposed records are not recoverable by cheap ensembles; the remaining levers are read latency (window rl) and cache capacity.**

The chained laptop waiters launched the overnight windows before the laptop died. Standard short-prompt gold is `9449ae5738`; rates below are tok/s, with r1/r2 unless r0 is explicitly included. Evidence is retained in `evidence/overnight/`.

Patched P8 at 8192 tokens gives md5 `5e7108fd8d` twice. **At 16k on the clean tree neither stock nor 0021 varied across three cold passes and both gave the same bytes; the model-free check (window o) remains the only demonstrated layout dependence. 0021 costs nothing (68.4 vs 68.7 tok/s) and stays a candidate for adoption as the prefill-testability guarantee; not urgent.** |
