The interpretation is an observer effect: the digest observer's eval callback disables CUDA graph capture and splits the backend graph, allowing the per-column exact dispatch to run. Without it, decode is replayed as a CUDA graph and bypasses the per-column paths: host synchronisation is not possible inside a captured region, so dispatch falls back to batched kernels. This explains why v2 exact mode, whose side effects also kept graphs off, matched end to end, while v3/v4 match only under the observer. The fde throughput figures therefore do not establish the cost of an exact full run at N>1. **v4 is exact at N=2 when the observer is on (both columns PASS) but the full run without the observer produces plain-batching bytes: the per-column paths are bypassed under CUDA graph replay. The earlier padding tie-order explanation is superseded by window fag below: selected membership is identical in every differing case, while the larger effective KV changes attention rounding. A client with >= 2048 valid keys has the same K in both cases. The fix is per-stream standalone extents and dispatch (0036 v4); fd9 measures it. Exact mode also slows the 2048-token prefill to ~50 tok/s from ~109; the cause is under investigation. **Warm cache and unequal prompts are exact for the long client in every batch shape; the remaining divergence is the short-context regime (valid keys < top_k) where the batched effective KV extent changes attention rounding despite identical selected membership (proven by fag). | fag (21:01) | Window fag (21:01), the flash-attention geometry probe, runs a standalone CUDA program linked against the production tree's ggml (`~/llama-k3-fd6/build-fd4`), beside the fd8 build, with no model or server. The [probe source](../tools/glm53/probe/fattn-geometry.cu) builds GLM DSA decode attention for ONE query token exactly as `glm-dsa.cpp` does: Q 576x1x64 heads, one F16 KV head, V aliasing K's first 512 channels, scale 1/16, production TOP_K/CONT and SET_ROWS selection, mask add, and F32 accumulation. Fixed pseudo-random Q/K/V are tested with KV extent E in {256, 512, 2048, 4096, 32768, 65536}, n_valid finite keys in {10, 138, 300} (the rest -INF), and K either the production rule min(E, 2048) or the standalone rule (n_valid padded to 256). Each of 34 configurations is compared bit for bit against E equal to the standalone extent, with identical selected membership verified. | The [evidence](evidence/glm-windows-2026-10-30/fag/legs-fag.txt) reports `SUMMARY configs=34 differing=12 membership=identical repeats=identical verdict=GEOMETRY_SENSITIVE`. For valid=138, E=2048/4096/32768/65536 with K=2048 differ from the E=256 baseline by max_abs ~1.6e-4; K=256 restores bit identity on the sparse path, but dense dispatch forces effective KV=E at E=512 and 2048, so lowering K there does not restore it. For valid=300, K=2048 differs from the E=512 baseline by ~9.2e-5, while K=512 is identical under the sparse path; dense E=2048 again forces effective KV=E. All valid=10 configurations match. With the same valid keys and selected set, changing effective KV can therefore change the FA output. This identifies the mechanism behind the short-prompt divergence at N>1: a batched ubatch gives the short stream the common extent's K=2048 instead of its standalone K=256, changing the kernel's partition/reduction over effective KV and its rounding; the long client (>= 2048 valid keys) keeps the same K and matches. The fix must give each stream its standalone extent and dispatch (0036 v4, window fd9). The padding tie-order explanation in fdw is superseded: membership was identical in every differing case. **Proven: the DSA decode attention output depends on the effective KV extent even with identical selected keys (12 of 34 configurations differ, membership identical). Per-stream standalone extents (0036 v4) are the required fix; fd9 measures it.** |

V4 N=2 versus N=1 has no compute divergence in either column (both PASS) under the observer; each column's shapes equal N=1's: `indexer_score` 256, `SET_ROWS` 1x256, `FLASH_ATTN_EXT` 512x64. The v3 control comparison reports divergences at `norm-10` / `norm-4`, an alignment artifact of its one-graph-per-sequence layout: the comparator numbers steps per graph. These are not evidence of a real v3 compute divergence. The v5 build passes, but the real leg dies at `ggml.c:1825 GGML_ASSERT(obj_new)`: the graph context runs out of node slots because v5's per-stream packing exceeds llama's max-nodes budget. There is no gold or throughput number. V4's geometry is right under the observer (no fusion), yet the real fused N=2 run in fd9 gave plain-batching bytes. Together with v2 (fusion off) being self-consistent and v3's per-sequence graphs being gold, this supports the reading that per-column exact dispatch does not engage in real fused runs: fused ops bypass the splitter, and N=1 stays gold because it needs no splitting. The v5 fused-site split is the candidate fix, but it crashed on the node budget. Next is a node-budget fix plus exact-dispatch counters printed by the engine, so a real run proves which path ran (fd11). **Under the observer v4 is exact and its attention geometry equals standalone; in real fused runs the per-column dispatch is not engaging. The fused-site split (0035/0036 v5) crashed on the graph node budget. Next: node budget + exact-dispatch counters (fd11). The only version gold at N>1 in real runs remains the per-sequence-graph v3, which does not batch.** |

All exact flags and `LLAMA_PREFILL_ISOLATE=1` are set; N=2 also uses `LLAMA_EXPERT_STORE_SPLIT_COLS=1`. The standard request (10-token prompt + 128 generated tokens) runs cold/prime/warm in three legs: N=1, N=2 fusion on, and N=2 with `GGML_CUDA_DISABLE_FUSION=1`, without the observer. | Evidence: [legs-fd11.txt](evidence/glm-windows-2026-10-30/fd11/legs-fd11.txt) and [runner56-fd11.txt](evidence/glm-windows-2026-10-30/fd11/runner56-fd11.txt). A first launch at 00:48 aborted on a missing staged evidence path; no engine run occurred. N=1 is gold (`9449ae5738` cold, `f675805e` prime/warm), at 4.95 tok/s warm aggregate. N=2 fusion on gives both clients `232e8f4e` cold and `74d484f8` prime/warm, NOT gold, at 6.74/6.74/7.11 aggregate tok/s. N=2 fusion off gives both clients `400f1b3bb1` cold and `7669259d` prime/warm, at 6.40/6.80/7.11 aggregate tok/s. The fused matvec sites split at N=2: `FUSED_Q split=508 batched=0`, `FUSED_ID_Q split=224 batched=0`. Batched `MUL_MAT`/`MUL_MAT_ID` calls at N=2 (1798/444) are exactly twice N=1's (899/222): the two prompts' prefill ubatches, as standalone. These are host-call counters, including capture and excluding replay. Exact dispatch therefore engages in real fused runs, refuting fd10's splitter-bypass reading. With fusion off, N=2 reproduces fusion-off N=1 bytes (`400f1b3bb1` is the N=1 fusion-off md5 measured in fdg), so exact mode is self-consistent across N when nothing is fused. With fusion on, N=2 equals plain batching byte for byte, pointing to a fused kernel outside the matvec sites (norm, add chain, rope+set_rows, router top-k, or unary) that computes differently with 2 rows than with 1. A per-fusion-class bisection, window fdb, is in design. Exact N=2 gives about 6.7–7.1 aggregate tok/s (+22–28 % over single-stream) with fusion on or off. **The per-column exact dispatch engages in real runs (counters); exact N=2 is bit-identical across N when fusion is off and gains +22-28 % aggregate. With fusion on one non-matvec fused kernel differs at 2 rows; bisection by fusion class next (fdb).** |

The standard request is the 10-token prompt plus 128 generated tokens, `temperature=0`, `seed=1`, ubatch 2048. All exact flags (`GGML_CUDA_EXACT_COLS=1 GGML_CUDA_MMVQ_EXACT_COLS=1 LLAMA_EXACT_ATTN_COLS=1 GGML_CUDA_ARGSORT_EXACT_ROWS=1`) plus `LLAMA_PREFILL_ISOLATE=1 LLAMA_EXPERT_STORE_SPLIT_COLS=1` are set. Disabling `rms_norm`, `rms_norm_rope`, `rope_set_rows`, `multi_add`, `multi_mul`, `unary_mul`, `matvec` or `shared_expert` alone has no effect: N=1 gold, N=2 `232e8f4e`. Disabling `moe_weighted_reduction` alone gives N=1 = N=2 = `f451f8a585` (EQUAL, not production gold). Disabling `topk_moe` alone gives N=1 `232e8f4e` (the N=2 fused bytes), N=2 `b5933beb` (plain batching's other column). Seven absent GLM graph classes were skipped: `rms_norm_scale`, `ssm_conv`, `gdn_cache`, `ssm_scan_cache`, `snake`, `relu_sqr`, `softcap`. **Culprit found: the fused MoE weighted reduction (with the top-k routing fusion). Every other fusion class is row-exact. Fix: per-token fused launch in exact mode (0035 v8, window fdr).** |

### Spark OS/driver/firmware update, 2026-11-01

The Spark OTA meta package moved **26.03.1 → 26.09.3** (513 packages). Driver **580.159.03 → 580.178.04**, kernel **6.17.0-1018 → 7.0.0-1024**, EC **0x03000302 → 0x03000d07**, SoC **0x0200980f → 0x02009e0f**, USB-PD **0x516 → 0x519**, and VBIOS **9A.0B.25 → 9A.0B.34**. The production image is unchanged: `g72518b8` = `:stable`. Evidence: [before/after checks](evidence/glm-windows-2026-10-30/ota/checks.txt), [pre-OTA packages](evidence/glm-windows-2026-10-30/ota/pre-ota-2026-11-01-packages.txt), [firmware update log](evidence/glm-windows-2026-10-30/ota/ota-2026-11-01-fwupd.log).

Output bytes are identical before and after: decode md5 `9449ae5738`, 2048-token prompt md5 `aa6d55b8e3`. Warm decode is unchanged at **5.71–5.80 tok/s**, versus **5.77** before. The first request after reboot ran at **5.01 tok/s** with an empty page cache. The 2048-token prefill improves from **105.8–106.7 to 109.9–110.4 tok/s (~+3.5 %)**; decode immediately after that prompt is **2.53/3.40**, versus **2.47/3.19 tok/s** before. These are platform-update checks on the unchanged production image.

Recorded rollback path: driver `580.159.03-1ubuntu1` remains in NVIDIA's CUDA apt repository, and kernel `6.17.0-1018` remains installed. Firmware is not rolled back.

Third-pass table continued after the platform update:

| window | candidate | result |
|---|---|---|

All exact flags (`GGML_CUDA_EXACT_COLS=1 GGML_CUDA_MMVQ_EXACT_COLS=1 LLAMA_EXACT_ATTN_COLS=1 GGML_CUDA_ARGSORT_EXACT_ROWS=1`) plus `LLAMA_PREFILL_ISOLATE=1 LLAMA_EXPERT_STORE_SPLIT_COLS=1` are set, with fusion enabled and `--batch-size 2048 --ubatch-size 2048`. The C64 48k prompt prefilled at ~113 tok/s beside the decoding client (the completed request reports 107.7 tok/s overall); that client's bytes stayed gold while decode stalled to 0.26 tok/s during prefill, consistent with prefill isolation. The standalone reference was killed at 81 % by the window's own 900 s server cap: two 48k prefills do not fit in 900 s, with no server error or memory pressure causing the stop (the client reports a missing final SSE event). Thus 2 x 64k preserves today's 65536-token context per request and gold bytes, at -4.5 % single-stream and +27 % aggregate with two users relative to production single-stream. At 2 x 128k, bytes depend on the configured context size even for a single sequence: the output is self-consistent across N but is a different gold; the op that reads the context size is not yet identified. It costs 9–19 % and runs memory below the valve reserve. **--parallel 2 with 65536 tokens per slot (ctx 131072, cache 78000 MiB) is gold and costs 4.5 % single-stream for +27 % with two users; 128k per slot changes the bytes (context-size-dependent gold) and costs 9-19 %.** |

The night's adoption decisions are 84000 MiB now (manifest change, rollout pending) and prefill bank v3 after its clean-tree confirmation (+47 % prefill, ~105 tok/s at 2k–8k; rollout pending). The gm rerun proves activation but is neutral to -0.5 %, so patch 0027 is not adopted; bg is dead and patch 0028 is rejected; dma closes the host-bank DMA-ring lever (3x slower than direct reads); the thp rerun above passed with the fixed checker and is a candidate after the prefill bank; z completed with c4 and is rejected (-7 %); the ready-flag idea is closed. The waiter loss cost the o2 runner log only; its per-pass results survived.

### Revisit when the bottleneck moves

Neutral is not dead: these are lossless and stay in the engine behind a flag, but measured neutral only because
something else dominated at the time. Re-test them after any change that shifts the per-token breakdown.

| change | why it was neutral | re-test when |
|---|---|---|
| Predictive prefetch (`LLAMA_EXPERT_STORE_PREFETCH=1`) | Spark: v2 (side reader, bounded, preemptible) is deployed, +12 %. GH200: v2 measured 2026-10-22 under the CPU split, -18 %: wrong guesses evict resident experts at 99 % residency and the predictor costs more GPU time than the reads it can hide | GH200: only if the miss rate rises (smaller cache) or the predictor becomes free |
| `GGML_CUDA_GRAPH_OPT=1` | parallel attention branches hidden behind the expert waits | GPU-bound phases (fewer misses, prefill) |
| Async host-bank hook / CUDA graphs | launch overhead was not the bottleneck | when GPU time per layer dominates |
| Eviction policy (ARC / LFU decay, simulated) | ~8 % fewer misses at the GH200's capacity | tighter caches (the Spark's 10 %) |
| Hugepage destinations for O_DIRECT (3.9 vs 4.5 ms per fill) | THP on host regions gave NaN on the GB10 | once that NaN is understood |
| io_uring on the GH200 | one submitter = one virtio queue | with multiple rings across CPUs |

### Open ideas (not yet measured)

- **GH200 prefill past the read ceiling.** With the lookahead, a 2048-token ubatch reads ~118 GB (every
  non-resident expert once) at the drive's 9.6 GB/s; reads per token halve with each doubling of the ubatch
  (4096: 186 tok/s measured), but the compute buffer doubles too and the HBM has no room for it next to a 24 GiB
  tier. A smaller HBM tier plus ubatch 4096 is a trade of decode speed (the tier's hits) for prefill speed: measure
  both before choosing. Also unmeasured: posting layer 1 of the next ubatch at the last layer (~1 %).
- **Prefill numerics that do not depend on the cache layout.** See the GH200 prefill section: the same prompt gives
  a different (equivalent) text depending on which slots hold the experts. Pin down the kernel (suspect: MMQ
  stream-k over the slot-ordered expert list) and, if cheap, make it order-independent, which would make every
  prefill change testable bit for bit. Window q reproduces this on the Spark with the same snapshot
  (0021 study); long-prompt md5s cannot gate prefill changes yet. Its cached round 2 also needs a server-log
  check for the unexplained slow decode before using the prompt-cache timing.
  See window o in the second-pass candidate table above: 0021 passes the model-free order check with decode gold and no measurable prefill cost at 2k; window o2 must confirm 16k/ubatch-2048 before adoption and re-baselining the long-prompt golds.
- **The misses' matmuls as their records land.** The split expert GEMV (2026-10-22) hides the resident experts'
  matmuls under the fill; the misses' still run after the last record. A per-id wait inside `mul_mat_vec_q`
  (each block spinning on its record's ready flag, set by the reader after the DMA) would hide all but the last
  record's share, ~0.2 ms per layer (~3 %), at the price of a kernel change and a mapped flag per slot.
  Measured in window z (13:29) above: -7 %, bytes identical; not adopted, idea closed.
- **A zero record the GPU caches.** The dummy rows of the split's late matmul cost real bandwidth (~28 us per id)
  because the GPU reads the host bank uncached; a zero record in device memory mapped into the same virtual range
  (CUDA VMM: `cuMemAddressReserve` + `cuMemMap`, host-NUMA allocations for the bank) would make them free and let
  the late matmul keep 16 rows without the synchronous excess.
- **A more precise predictor still.** Input 2 has precision 0.675; the rest is the next layer's attention, which is
  only known after it runs. The oracle replay (2.39 records in the self-prefetch fill vs 3.9) is the ceiling.
  Window p measured inputs 4 and 5 slower than production input 3; input 5 increases candidate recall but
  reads many more unused predictions. A new predictor needs to improve useful reads as well as recall.
  The real-trace study keeps predictor input 3: the exposed records are not recoverable by cheap ensembles, leaving read latency (window rl) and cache capacity as the remaining levers.
- **The rest of the Spark perf-hunt list.** ~~Shared expert and routed_down under the I/O wait (self-prefetch with
  the real ids, est. +6 %)~~: the 0022 study found the shared FFN already overlaps the real-lookup wait through
  0005's `moe_overlap` with predictive prefetch input 3; that estimate is already spent (window sh is confirmation only).
  The poller falling asleep inside the GPU gap was patch 0012 (hook lean, adopted). Still open: I/O and poller threads on the X925 cores (+2 %, patch 0023 queued as window af).
  The reads now run at the drive's ceiling, so more overlap is the remaining lever on the read side.
- **Why THP on the host regions gives NaN on the GB10.** Fixing it would bring back hugepage destinations
  (3.9 vs 4.5 ms per fill), but only if the pages also survive the load's reclaim.
- **Frequency-aware eviction for the host cache.** The routing skew that makes the HBM tier work should also
  help a policy like LFU or ARC beat plain LRU, which matters most on the Spark's 10 % cache. Window r
  attempted LFU (0018), but both LFU legs lacked valid timed responses; investigate and rerun before
  drawing a performance conclusion.
- **Q6_K GEMV for Hopper.** The TMA kernel (kept, +2 % end to end) brought the big shapes to 2.8-3.4 TB/s; what
  is left is the fused shared-expert gate/up (still stock, the TMA version measured slower in the server) and the
  small shapes (attn_q_a, kv_a_mqa, ssm_f_a at 0.2-1.5 TB/s, launch-bound). Each variant listed in the 2026-10-26
  section is already measured; don't repeat them.
- **15+ tok/s on the GH200.** Only path found so far: all experts resident in memory, for example a second
  GH200 with expert parallelism.
